\displaystyle\geqslant\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{1}{2}\mathds{1}_{\frac{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}|\widehat{x}_{T}]}{Z_{T}}\leqslant\frac{c_{3}}{2}}}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left[{\frac{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}|\widehat{x}_{T}]}{Z_{T}}\leqslant\frac{c_{3}}{2}}\right]\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[B_{2}].

Hence we can bound the second term

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}}{Z_{T}}\frac{dp_{T}}{d\rho_{t}}(\widehat{x}_{T})\mathds{1}_{G_{1}\cap B_{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}}{Z_{T}}|\widehat{x}_{T}}\right]\frac{dp_{T}}{d\rho_{T}}(\widehat{x}_{T})\mathds{1}_{G_{1}\cap B_{2}}}\right]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{dp_{T}}{d\widehat{p}_{T}}(\widehat{x}_{T})\mathds{1}_{G_{1}\cap B_{2}}}\right]\leqslant L\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[B_{2}]\leqslant 2L\varepsilon_{3}.

Finally, note that under
G_{1}\cap G_{2}
,

\frac{dp_{T}}{d\rho_{T}}=\frac{dp_{T}}{d\widehat{p}_{T}}\big/\frac{d\rho_{T}}{d\widehat{p}_{T}}\leqslant\frac{2L}{c_{3}}
, so
for the third term, by assumption 2

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}}{Z_{T}}\frac{dp_{T}}{d\rho_{t}}(\widehat{x}_{T})\mathds{1}_{G_{1}\cap G_{2}\cap B_{3}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2L}{c_{3}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}}{Z_{T}}\mathds{1}_{B_{3}}}\right]\leqslant\frac{2L\varepsilon_{1}}{c_{3}}.

Putting everything together,

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}}{Z_{T}}\frac{dp_{T}}{d\rho_{T}}(\widehat{x}_{T})\mathds{1}_{B_{1}\cup B_{2}\cup B_{3}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{3}+2L\varepsilon_{3}+\frac{2L\varepsilon_{1}}{c_{3}}=\varepsilon

by choosing
\varepsilon_{3}=\frac{\varepsilon}{6L}
and
\varepsilon_{1}=\frac{c_{3}\varepsilon}{6L}
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}}{Z_{T}}\frac{dp_{T}}{d\rho_{T}}(\widehat{x}_{T})\wedge\frac{2C_{1}L}{c_{3}}}\right]\geqslant\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}}{Z_{T}}\frac{dp_{T}}{d\rho_{T}}\mathds{1}_{G_{1}\cap G_{2}\cap G_{3}}}\right]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{dp_{T}}{d\widehat{p}_{T}}(x_{T})}\right]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[{\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}}{Z_{T}}\frac{dp_{T}}{d\rho_{T}}\mathds{1}_{B_{1}\cup B_{2}\cup B_{3}}}\right]\geqslant 1-\varepsilon.

The first part now follows from
Lemma
3.8
, with
\varepsilon_{\textup{sample}}=\delta=\varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}}=0
. Here, we are considering path measures
P
and
Q
where

Q=\widehat{p}_{[0,T]}
, the measure of
(\widehat{x}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
, and
P
is such that
\frac{dP}{dQ}((x_{t})_{t\in[0,T]})=\frac{dp_{T}}{d\widehat{p}_{T}}(x_{T})
, and then taking the marginal distribution of
x_{T}
. Note we allow probability
\varepsilon
of failure (in the quantile estimate or rejection sampling taking too many trials).

For the second part, let
\widetilde{Q}=\widetilde{p}_{[0,T]}
be the path measure of
(\widetilde{x}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
. Let

\widetilde{R}=e^{\widetilde{w}_{T}}\widetilde{R}_{2}
, and
R=e^{\widehat{w}_{T}}R_{2}
.
Note that assumption
4
gives assumption
1
of
Lemma
3.8
with
\varepsilon_{\textup{sample}}=\varepsilon_{4}
. Consider the events

\displaystyle G_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\frac{\widetilde{R}_{2}}{R_{2}}\in[0,e^{\varepsilon_{4}/2}]}\right\}\cap\left\{{|\widetilde{w}_{T}-\widehat{w}_{T}|\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon_{4}}{2}}\right\}\subseteq\left\{{\frac{\widetilde{R}}{R}\in[0,e^{\varepsilon_{4}}]}\right\}.

\displaystyle G_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\frac{\widetilde{R}_{2}}{R_{2}}\in[e^{-\varepsilon_{4}/2},e^{\varepsilon_{4}/2}]}\right\},

and note
G_{1}^{\prime}\cap G_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq\left\{{\frac{\widetilde{R}}{R}\in[e^{-\varepsilon_{4}},e^{\varepsilon_{4}}]}\right\}
. It remains to check assumption
3
of
Lemma
3.8
. We check

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}({G_{1}^{\prime}}^{c})

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left({\frac{\widetilde{R}_{2}(\widetilde{x}_{T})}{R_{2}(\widetilde{x}_{T})}\nin[0,e^{\varepsilon_{4}/2}]}\right)+\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left({|\widetilde{w}_{T}-\widehat{w}_{T}|>\frac{\varepsilon_{4}}{2}}\right)\leqslant_{\eqref{i:je-ewt},\eqref{i:je-ert}(a)}\frac{\varepsilon_{4}}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{4}}{2}=\varepsilon_{4}

\displaystyle P({G_{1}^{\prime}}^{c})

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left({\frac{\widetilde{R}_{2}(\widetilde{x}_{T})}{R_{2}(\widetilde{x}_{T})}\nin[e^{-\varepsilon_{4}/2},e^{\varepsilon_{4}/2}]}\right)\leqslant_{\eqref{i:je-ert}(b)}\varepsilon.

Thus the assumption holds with
\varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}}=\delta_{1}=\delta_{2}=\varepsilon_{4}
. The final TV error of the sampled distribution
\widehat{P}
is

\operatorname{TV}(\widehat{P},P)\leqslant\varepsilon_{\textup{weight}}+\delta_{2}+384C_{1}^{\prime}C_{2}^{\prime}(\delta_{1}+\varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{sample}})\leqslant\varepsilon+\varepsilon+384{C_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}(\varepsilon_{4}+\varepsilon_{4}+\varepsilon_{4})=5\varepsilon.

Allowing probability
\varepsilon
of failure then gives the result.

Having a bounded exponential moment on
[-1,\lambda_{+}>1]
is sufficient to satisfy assumptions 2 and 3 of
Lemma
3.9
.

Lemma 3.10.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Suppose that
W
is a random variable such that for some
\lambda_{+}>1
, for
\lambda\in\{-1,1,\lambda_{+}\}
, we have

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{\lambda W-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\lambda W]}]\leqslant K_{\lambda}.

Then letting
Z=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{W}]
,

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{W}\mathds{1}_{e^{W}/Z>C_{1}}]

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon_{1}Z

\displaystyle\text{when }C_{1}

\displaystyle=\left({\frac{K_{\lambda_{+}}}{\varepsilon_{1}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\lambda_{+}-1}}

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left[{\frac{e^{W}}{Z}\leqslant c_{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon_{3}

\displaystyle\text{when }c_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{-1}K_{1}}{\varepsilon_{3}}.

Proof 9
.

By Jensen’s inequality and the mgf bound,

\displaystyle 1\leqslant\frac{Z}{e^{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}W}}\leqslant K_{1}.

Then

\displaystyle K_{\lambda_{+}}e^{\lambda_{+}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}W}

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{\lambda_{+}W}]\geqslant(ZC_{1})^{\lambda_{+}-1}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{W}\mathds{1}_{e^{W}/Z\geqslant C_{1}}]

\displaystyle\implies K_{\lambda_{+}}C_{1}^{-(\lambda_{+}-1)}Z

\displaystyle\geqslant K_{\lambda_{+}}C_{1}^{-(\lambda_{+}-1)}e^{\lambda_{+}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}W}Z^{-(\lambda_{+}-1)}\geqslant\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{W}\mathds{1}_{e^{W}/Z\geqslant C_{1}}].

Taking
C_{1}
as defined gives the first inequality.

For the second inequality,

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}(e^{W}/Z\leqslant c_{3})=\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}(e^{-W}\geqslant Zc_{3}^{-1})\leqslant\frac{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{-W}]}{Z^{-1}c_{3}^{-1}}\leqslant\frac{K_{-1}e^{-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}W}}{Z^{-1}c_{3}^{-1}}\leqslant K_{1}K_{-1}c_{3}.

Taking
c_{3}
as defined gives the second inequality.

3.5.
Discretization analysis

We need to consider the discretization of the SDE for the tilt
y_{t}
, as well as the ODE for the weights
w_{t}
.
First, we use the standard result that the path measure of the interpolated discrete SDE is close to that of the actual SDE by Girsanov’s theorem. Then for the weights, we conduct the analysis on the interpolated discrete SDE, so the analysis reduces to bounding Brownian motion.
This will show that conditions (4) and (5) in
Lemma
3.9
are satisfied.

Lemma 3.11
(SDE discretization bound, cf.
[
47
, Lemma 4.4.1]
,
[
50
]
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Fix a stepsize
h>0
and let
t^{-}=h\left\lfloor\frac{t}{h}\right\rfloor
.
Suppose that
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is
L
-Lipschitz.
Consider the two SDE’s in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle dy_{t}

\displaystyle=f(y_{t})dt+dB_{t}

\displaystyle d\widetilde{y}_{t}

\displaystyle=f(\widetilde{y}_{t^{-}})dt+dB_{t},

where
y_{0},\widetilde{y}_{0}\sim\rho_{0}
.
Let
T=Nh
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{KL}(\mathcal{L}((\widetilde{y}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]})\|\mathcal{L}((y_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}))

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{L^{2}h^{3}}{6}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\|{f(\widetilde{y}_{kh})}\right\|^{2}+\frac{TL^{2}nh}{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant TL^{2}\left({\frac{h^{2}M^{2}}{6}+\frac{nh}{2}}\right)

where the last inequality holds if
\left\|{f}\right\|_{\infty}\leqslant M
. If
h\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{3/2}\varepsilon}{\sqrt{T}LM}\wedge\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2nTL^{2}}
, then
\operatorname{TV}(\mathcal{L}((\widetilde{y}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}),\mathcal{L}((y_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}))\leqslant\varepsilon
.

Proof 10
.

Note that while
[
47
, Lemma 4.4.1]
specifically considers Langevin diffusion, where
f=\nabla V
, the argument extends to any Lipschitz
f
. Since the diffusion term is
dB_{t}
rather than
\sqrt{2}dB_{t}
, the error is scaled by
\frac{1}{2}
.
The last statement holds by Pinsker’s inequality,
\operatorname{TV}(\mu,\nu)\leqslant\sqrt{2\operatorname{KL}(\mu\|\nu)}
.

Lemma 3.12
(Discretization for JE weights under interpolated discrete SDE)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Fix a stepsize
h>0
and let
t^{-}=h\left\lfloor\frac{t}{h}\right\rfloor
.
Consider a discrete-time process

\widetilde{y}_{t+h}=\widetilde{y}_{t}+h\widehat{f}(\widetilde{y}_{t})+\sqrt{h}\xi_{t},\quad\xi_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n}),\quad\text{for }t=kh,\,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Consider the continuous-time interpolation of this process,

d\widetilde{y}_{t}=\widehat{f}(\widetilde{y}_{t^{-}})dt+dB_{t}

and consider

d\widehat{w}_{t}=\omega(\widetilde{y}_{t})dt.

Consider the discretization of this ODE,

\widetilde{w}_{t+h}=\widetilde{w}_{t}+h\omega(\widetilde{y}_{t}),\quad\text{for }t=kh,\,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Suppose
\left\|{\widehat{f}}\right\|_{\infty}\leqslant M
.
Then for some constant
C
, for
T=Nh
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(3.25)

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left({|\widetilde{w}_{T}-\widehat{w}_{T}|\geqslant L_{\omega}\left({\frac{TMh}{2}+\frac{Th^{1/2}n^{1/2}}{2^{1/2}}+\sqrt{Tn}hu}\right)}\right)\leqslant e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{C}}

Thus for
h\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2L_{\omega}TM}\wedge\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2L_{\omega}^{2}T^{2}n}\wedge\frac{1}{4L_{\omega}\sqrt{TnC\log\left({\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}}
, we have
\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left({|\widetilde{w}_{T}-\widehat{w}_{T}|\geqslant\varepsilon}\right)\leqslant\delta
.

Proof 11
.

Note

\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{y}_{t^{-}}+(t-t^{-})\widehat{f}(\widetilde{y}_{t^{-}})+(B_{t}-B_{t^{-}})

\displaystyle\left\|{\widetilde{y}_{t}-\widetilde{y}_{t^{-}}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant(t-t^{-})M+\left\|{B_{t}-B_{t^{-}}}\right\|

We first bound

\displaystyle|\widetilde{w}_{T}-\widehat{w}_{T}|

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}|\omega(\widetilde{y}_{t})-\omega(\widetilde{y}_{t^{-}})|dt

\displaystyle\leqslant L_{\omega}\int_{0}^{T}\left\|{\widetilde{y}_{t}-\widetilde{y}_{t^{-}}}\right\|dt

\displaystyle\leqslant L_{\omega}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{kh}^{(k+1)h}(t-kh)M+\left\|{B_{t}-B_{kh}}\right\|dt

\displaystyle\leqslant L_{\omega}\left({\frac{TMh}{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{kh}^{(k+1)h}\left\|{B_{t}-B_{kh}}\right\|dt}\right)

The second term is a sum of
N
iid copies of the random variable
\int_{0}^{h}\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|dt
. Note by Jensen’s inequality that

\displaystyle e^{\lambda\int_{0}^{h}\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|dt}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{h}\int_{0}^{h}e^{\lambda h\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|}dt.

Note that

\left\|{\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}=\left\|{\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|^{2}}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim tn
as
\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|^{2}
is
t
times a
\chi_{n}^{2}
random variable, which is a sum of
n
iid random variables satisfying
\left\|{X}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}=O(1)
.
Hence
\left\|{\int_{0}^{h}\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|dt-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{h}\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|dt}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\left\|{h\left\|{B_{h}}\right\|}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{3}n
.
Note

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{h}\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|dt\leqslant h^{1/2}\left({\int_{0}^{h}\left\|{B_{t}}\right\|^{2}dt}\right)^{1/2}\leqslant h^{1/2}\left({\int_{0}^{h}tn\,dt}\right)=\sqrt{\frac{h^{3}n}{2}}.

Hence we obtain (
3.25
).

4.
Estimating the covariance of tilted Gibbs measures via the TAP Hessian

Given a tilt
y_{t}
, assuming that
m_{t}
is a stationary point of the TAP free energy
m\mapsto\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\beta A,m,y_{t})
, one expects that with high probability over
A
and
y_{t}
sampled from (SL), that

\mathop{{}\bm{\mathrm{Cov}}}(\mu_{\beta A,y_{t}})\approx\left(\nabla^{2}_{m}\mathcal{F}_{\,\mathsf{TAP}}\left(\beta A,m_{t},y_{t}\right)\right)^{-1}\,.

Differentiating explicitly, the Hessian of the TAP free energy (evaluated at magnetization
m_{t}
) is

-\beta A-\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}+D(m_{t})+\beta^{2}\left(1-\frac{\langle m_{t},m_{t}\rangle}{n}\right)I_{n}-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}m_{t}m_{t}^{\mathsf{T}}\,,

where
D(m)
is the diagonal matrix with
D(m)_{ii}=\frac{1}{1-m_{i}^{2}}
.

The true covariance matrix for the planted SK model with a SL tilt is given as
P(A,y_{t}):=\mathop{{}\bm{\mathrm{Cov}}}(G_{A,y_{t},\beta})
. Furthermore, the SL process runs with a SDE that satisfies the following equivalent rewrite,

x_{0}\sim G_{A,\beta},\,\,\,\,\,y_{t}=tx_{0}+B_{t},\,\,B_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n})\,.

We are interested in bounding the following quantity

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A,B_{t}}\left[\left\lVert{\widehat{Q}^{-1}P-I_{n}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right]\leqslant O_{\beta}(1)\,,

for every fixed choice of
x_{0}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}
. At that point, the contiguity between the planted model and the original model
[
9
, §2]
yields that a
O_{\beta}(1)
bound also holds for the original model under the ASL process. The main result of this section is a rigorous proof of the aforementioned desired bound.

4.1.
Gibbs measures, Gaussian processes, and interpolations

The results in this section will heavily use certain facts about the derivatives of Gibbs measures with quadratic interactions and Gaussian processes. These facts (given below) primarily memorize the fact that the derivative of moments of Gibbs measures leads to larger (explicit) moments, while the derivatives of Gaussian processes are evaluated using Stein’s lemma.

The other critical fact that will be used (in various forms) in
§
5.4
is that the time derivative of a time interpolated Gaussian processes can be expressed in terms of its second spatial derivatives (resembling the behavior of the heat equation PDE).

Basic analytic properties of Gibbs measures

Below we state two elementary propositions. The first computes the derivative of the tilted Gibbs measure with respect to the interactions
A_{i,j}
, and the second extends this to obtain derivatives of the first two moments of the Gibbs measure.

Proposition 4.1
(Derivatives of the tilted Gibbs measure)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let the tilted partition function be

Z_{A,\beta,x_{0}}:=\sum_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}e^{\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i,j}A_{i,j}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}+\left\langle\sigma,y_{t}\right\rangle}\,.

Then, the derivative with respect to the interaction variable
A_{i,j}
is given as

\displaystyle\partial_{A_{i,j}}G_{A,\beta,x_{0}}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\partial_{A_{i,j}}\left(\frac{e^{H(\sigma)}}{Z_{A,\beta,x_{0}}}\right)=\frac{e^{H(\sigma)}\beta/2\left(Z_{A,\beta,x_{0}}\left(\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\right)-\sum_{\tau}\left(\tau_{i}\tau_{j}\right)e^{H(\tau)}\right)}{Z^{2}_{A,\beta,x_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta}{2}G_{A,\beta,x_{0}}(\sigma)\left(\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}-\left\langle\tau_{i}\tau_{j}\right\rangle_{G_{A,\beta,x_{0}}}\right)\,.

Proof 12
.

Treat
A_{i,j}
as a variable and apply product rule followed by an elementary factorization argument to rewrite the Gibbs average.

Proposition 4.2
(Derivatives of first and second moments under the symmetric convention)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
A=A^{\mathsf{T}}

and consider the Gibbs measure
G_{A,\beta,y_{t}}
on
\{\pm 1\}^{n}
with Hamiltonian

H(\sigma)\;:=\;\frac{\beta}{2}\,\sigma^{\mathsf{T}}A\sigma\;+\;\frac{\beta^{2}}{2n}\;+\;\langle y_{t},\sigma\rangle,\qquad G_{A,\beta,y_{t}}(\sigma)=\frac{e^{H(\sigma)}}{Z}.

Write
\langle\cdot\rangle
for expectation under
G_{A,\beta,y_{t}}
. For
k<\ell
let

\partial_{k,\ell}:=\partial/\partial A_{k\ell}
denote differentiation with respect to the

independent
upper-triangular GOE entry (so
A_{\ell k}=A_{k\ell}
), and let
\partial_{k,k}:=\partial/\partial A_{kk}
.

Define centered spins and centered “edge” observables by

m_{i}:=\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle,\qquad\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}:=\sigma_{i}-m_{i},\qquad\widetilde{\sigma_{ab}}:=\sigma_{a}\sigma_{b}-\langle\sigma_{a}\sigma_{b}\rangle.

Then for every
k<\ell
, every
i,j\in[n]
, and every
k_{i},k_{\ell}\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{k,\ell}\,\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle^{k_{i}}

\displaystyle=(2\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle)^{k_{i}-1}\,\beta\,\big\langle\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\,\widetilde{\sigma_{k\ell}}\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\partial_{k,\ell}\,\langle\sigma_{\ell}\rangle^{k_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=(2\langle\sigma_{\ell}\rangle)^{k_{\ell}-1}\,\beta\,\big\langle\widetilde{\sigma}_{\ell}\,\widetilde{\sigma_{k\ell}}\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\partial_{k,\ell}\,\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\beta\,\big\langle\widetilde{\sigma_{ij}}\,\widetilde{\sigma_{k\ell}}\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\partial_{k,\ell}\,\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{\ell}\rangle

\displaystyle=\beta\,\big\langle\widetilde{\sigma_{i\ell}}\,\widetilde{\sigma_{k\ell}}\big\rangle.

Moreover, for every
k\in[n]
and every observable
F(\sigma)
one has
\partial_{k,k}\langle F\rangle=0

(in particular
\partial_{k,k}\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle^{k_{i}}=0
and
\partial_{k,k}\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle=0
).

Proof.

For
k<\ell
, the symmetric convention gives

\partial_{k,\ell}H(\sigma)=\beta\,\sigma_{k}\sigma_{\ell}.

Hence for any observable
F
,

\partial_{k,\ell}\langle F\rangle=\big\langle(F-\langle F\rangle)\,\partial_{k,\ell}H\big\rangle=\beta\,\big\langle(F-\langle F\rangle)\,\widetilde{\sigma_{k\ell}}\big\rangle.

Taking
F=\sigma_{i}
yields
\partial_{k,\ell}\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle=\beta\langle\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\,\widetilde{\sigma_{k\ell}}\rangle
,
and the case
\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle^{k_{i}}
follows by the chain rule (noting that for
k_{i}\in\{1,2\}
,

k_{i}\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle^{k_{i}-1}=(2\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle)^{k_{i}-1}
). Taking
F=\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}

gives the stated centered formula for
\partial_{k,\ell}\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle
, and similarly for
F=\sigma_{i}\sigma_{\ell}
.
Finally, for diagonal entries
A_{kk}
one has
\partial_{k,k}H(\sigma)=\frac{\beta}{2}\sigma_{k}^{2}=\frac{\beta}{2}
, which is constant in
\sigma
,
so
\partial_{k,k}\langle F\rangle=\langle(F-\langle F\rangle)\,\frac{\beta}{2}\rangle=0
for all
F
.
∎

Extending the two propositions above allows for the computation of the
4
-tensor which gives the Hessian of the Gibbs covariance matrix for a planted (and tilted) SK model. This turns out to be of crucial importance in evaluating the symmetries that help bound the terms that show up in the proof of
Theorem
4.10
.

We now state a proposition that explicitly writes the magnetization of a coordinate
i\in[n]
under the Gibbs measure
G(A,tx_{0}+B_{t},\beta)
for a
fixed
instance of the disorder.

Lemma 4.3
(Magnetization under the tilted SK model)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

For every
i\in[n]
, a
fixed
choice of
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, and

y_{t}=tx_{0}+B_{t}\,,

where
B_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,t)
independently for every
t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geqslant 0}
, the following holds

(m_{t})_{i}:=\left\langle\sigma_{i}\right\rangle_{G(A,y_{t},\beta)}=\left\langle\tanh\left(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij}\sigma_{j}+t(x_{0})_{i}+(B_{t})_{i}\right)\right\rangle\in_{\text{a.s.}}(-1,1)\,.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by direct computation.

\displaystyle\left\langle\sigma_{i}\right\rangle_{G(A,y_{t},\beta)}=\sum_{\sigma}\sigma_{i}\frac{e^{\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k<\ell}A_{k\ell}\sigma_{k}\sigma_{\ell}+t\langle x_{0},\sigma\rangle+\langle B_{t},\sigma\rangle}}{\sum_{\tau}e^{\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k<\ell}A_{k\ell}\tau_{k}\tau_{\ell}+t\langle x_{0},\tau\rangle+\langle B_{t},\tau\rangle}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma_{\setminus\{i\}}}\sum_{\sigma_{i}}\sigma_{i}\frac{e^{\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k<\ell,k\neq i,\ell\neq i}A_{k\ell}\sigma_{k}\sigma_{\ell}+t\langle(x_{0})_{\setminus\{i\}},\sigma_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}\rangle+\langle(B_{t})_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}},\sigma_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}\rangle+\sigma_{i}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij\sigma_{j}}+t(x_{0})_{i}\sigma_{i}+(B_{t})_{i}\sigma_{i}}}{\sum_{\tau_{\setminus\{i\}}}\sum_{\tau_{i}}e^{\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k<\ell,k\neq i,\ell\neq i}A_{k\ell}\tau_{k}\tau_{\ell}+t\langle(x_{0})_{\setminus\{i\}},\tau_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}\rangle+\langle(B_{t})_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}},\tau_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}\rangle+\tau_{i}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij\tau_{j}}+t(x_{0})_{i}\tau_{i}+(B_{t})_{i}\tau_{i}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}}\left(\frac{e^{\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k<\ell,k\neq i,\ell\neq i}A_{k\ell}\sigma_{k}\sigma_{\ell}+t\langle(x_{0})_{\setminus\{i\}},\sigma_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}\rangle+\langle(B_{t})_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}},\sigma_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}\rangle}}{\sum_{\tau_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}}e^{\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k<\ell,k\neq i,\ell\neq i}A_{k\ell}\tau_{k}\tau\ell+t\langle(x_{0})_{\setminus\{i\}},\tau_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}\rangle+\langle(B_{t})_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}},\tau_{\setminus\left\{i\right\}}\rangle}2\cosh\left(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\neq j}A_{ij}\tau_{j}+t(x_{0})_{i}+(B_{t})_{i}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\,\,\,\,\,\left(2\sinh\left(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\neq j}A_{ij}\sigma_{j}+t(x_{0})_{i}+(B_{t})_{i}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\tanh\left(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij}\sigma_{j}+t(x_{0})_{i}+(B_{t})_{i}\right)\right\rangle\,.\qed

Gaussian and sub-Gaussian processes

To make explicit computations about the covariances under the Gibbs measure of the tilted model will require the following mild generalizations of Stein-type lemmata.

Proposition 4.4
(Stein’s lemma for uncentered Gaussian processes)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}\in C^{1}
be a function with slower than exponential growth.
Then, for every
i\in[n]
,

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(\mu,\Sigma)}\left[(g_{i}-\mu_{i})f(g)\right]=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Sigma_{ij}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{g_{j}}f(g)\right]\,.

Proposition 4.5
(Stein’s lemma with Gibbs averages
[
97
, Mild generalization of Lemma 1.1]
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}\in C^{1}
be a function with slower than exponential growth. Let
\{X(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}
and
\{Y(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}
be (possibly uncentered) Gaussian processes with a Gibbs measure
G_{n,\beta,Y}
defined over the process
Y
at inverse temperature
\beta
.

Then,

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle f\big(X(\sigma)-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[X(\sigma)]\big)\right\rangle_{G_{n,\beta,Y}}\right]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle f\big(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[X(\sigma)Y(\sigma)\right]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[X(\sigma)Y(\sigma^{\prime})\right]\big)\right\rangle_{G^{\otimes 2}_{n,\beta,Y}}\right]\,.

Guerra-type interpolations, overlaps and the cavity method

To obtain overlap-concentration for the planted SK model at high-temperature, interpolating all spins
simultaneously
via a Guerra interpolation is critical.

Definition 4.6

(Guerra-type interpolation between two independent Gaussian processes)
.

Let
\{H_{0}(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
and
\{H_{1}(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
be two independent Gaussian processes. Then, the Guerra-interpolated Gaussian process
\{H_{t}(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
is given as

(4.1)

H_{t}(\sigma)=\sqrt{1-t}H_{0}(\sigma)+\sqrt{t}H_{1}(\sigma)\,,

for
t\in[0,1]
.

See
[
7
, §5]
for the concrete instantiation of a Guerra interpolation for the planted SK model. Noting that
\{H_{t}(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
is a Gaussian process for every
t\in[0,1]
and using
Proposition
4.5
leads to control of the time-derivative when interpolating between the free-energies of two Gaussian processes.

Lemma 4.7
(Time-derivative of
C^{2}
functions in a Guerra-type interpolation,
[
109
, Lemma 1.3.1]
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
\{H_{t}(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
be a Guerra-interpolated Gaussian process as defined in
Definition
4.6
. Then, given a function
f:H_{t}(\cdot)\to\mathbb{R}
, the following holds

\frac{d}{dt}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[f(H_{t}(\cdot))\right]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma}\left(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[H_{1}(\sigma)H_{1}(\tau)\right]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[H_{0}(\sigma)H_{0}(\tau)\right]\right)\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{x_{\sigma}}\partial_{x_{\tau}}f(H_{t}(x))\right]\,.

Lemma
4.7
is used to derive the free energy for the planted SK model in the replica-symmetric regime in
[
7
]
. The replica-symmetric regime for the planted SK model is characterized by magnetization and overlap concentration.

Definition 4.8

(Overlap and magnetization moment generation functions)
.

Let
\{H(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
be a Gaussian process. Then, given a fixed
\beta>0
and any
fixed

k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, the moments of the deviations of the magnetization and overlap parameters of this process are given as

(4.2)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{H}\left[\left\langle\left(m-R_{\sigma,\vec{1}}\right)^{2k}\right\rangle\right]\,,

(4.3)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{H}\left[\left\langle\left(q-R_{1,2}\right)^{2k}\right\rangle\right]\,.

These quantities are bounded for the planted SK model when
\beta<1
via an analysis of some fixed-point equations, followed by an appeal to the Nishimori identity (
Lemma
5.32
) and a “mapping” of our model to the Guerra-interpolated model in
[
7
, §3]
.

Studying the precise limiting values of functions of the overlap arrays requires computing the
exact
limits for some of its statistics. This is accomplished via a cavity interpolation.

Definition 4.9

(Cavity interpolation for the planted SK model, Modification of
[
39
, §2]
)
.

Consider the Hamiltonian of the planted model (with the functions of the last site isolated):

\beta H(\sigma)=\sqrt{\frac{(n-1)}{n}}\beta H_{n-1}(\rho)+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2n}+\frac{\beta}{2}A_{nn}+\sigma_{n}\left(g(\rho)+(y_{t})_{n}\right)\,,

where
\rho=\left(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{n-1}\right)
,
(y_{t})_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(t,\beta^{2}t)
,
A_{nn}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{2}{n}\right)
and

g(\rho):=\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\sigma_{i}+\beta\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}A_{i,n}\sigma_{i}\,.

The interpolation that smoothly decouples a
(n-1)
-dimensional configuration
\rho
and a
n
-dimensional configuration
\sigma=\rho\cdot\sigma_{n}
for the Hamiltonian
H_{t}(\sigma)
with magnetization
m\in[-1,1]
and overlap
q\in[0,1]
is defined as

\displaystyle\beta H_{t}(\sigma):=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(n-1)}{n}}\beta H_{n-1}(\rho)+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2n}+\frac{\beta}{2}A_{nn}+\left\{g_{t}(\rho)+(y_{t})_{n}\right\}\sigma_{n}\,,

where

(4.4)

g_{t}(\rho):=\beta^{2}\left(\frac{t}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\sigma_{i}+(1-t)m\right)+\beta\left(\sqrt{t}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}A_{in}\sigma_{i}+\sqrt{1-t}\sqrt{q}\,h\right)\,,

for
t\in[0,1]
and
h\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

This cavity interpolation in
Definition
4.9
is used to extend the ideas in
[
109
, §1.6 & §1.8]
to obtain various limiting estimates for the planted SK model (
§
5.4
).

4.2.
Covariance estimation error via moments of overlaps of the planted Gibbs measure

The main theorem of this section is a bound on the Frobenius norm that measures the error between the ideal process, driven by the ideal covariance, and the algorithmic process, driven by the covariance obtained as the inverse Hessian of the TAP free energy for the (planted) SK model under SL tilt.

Theorem 4.10
(Frobenius norm error between PHA & ASL-tilted SK covariance matrices)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
D(x)=\diag\left(\frac{1}{1-x^{2}}\right)
for any
x\in(-1,1)^{n}
and

\widehat{Q}(m_{t})=\left(\beta^{2}\left(1-\frac{\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}}{n}\right)I_{n}-\beta A-\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}+D(m_{t})-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}m_{t}m_{t}^{\mathsf{T}}\right)^{-1}\,,

where
t\in[0,T]
, and

m_{t}=\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle\,.

Fix
x_{0}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}
and let

P=P(A,y_{t})=\mathop{{}\bm{\mathrm{Cov}}}(G_{A,y_{t},\beta})\,.

Then,

(4.5)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A,B_{t}}\left\lVert{\widehat{Q}^{-1}P-I_{n}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}=O_{\beta,t}(1)\,.

Proof 13
.

Set
S=\beta^{2}(1-\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}/n)I_{n}+D(m_{t})-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}m_{t}m_{t}^{\mathsf{T}}
. Then, by simple linear algebra,

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A,B_{t}}\left\lVert{\widehat{Q}^{-1}P-I_{n}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A,B_{t}}\left[\left\lVert{\widehat{Q}^{-1}P}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A,B_{t}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}(\widehat{Q}^{-1}P)\right]+n=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P(\widehat{Q}^{-1})^{2}P]\right]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[\widehat{Q}^{-1}P\right]\right]+n

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[P\widehat{Q}^{-2}P\right]\right]+2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP]+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}P]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[SP\right]\right]+n

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[PS^{2}P\right]\right]+\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[PA^{2}P\right]\right]-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[PSAP\right]\right]+\frac{\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})^{2}P]]-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[PS(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})P]

\displaystyle+\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[PA(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})P\right]+2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}\left[AP\right]+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}P]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[SP\right]\right]+n

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[PS^{2}P\right]\right]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[SP\right]\right]+n}_{=\mathcal{E}_{D}}+\underbrace{\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[PA^{2}P\right]\right]-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[PSAP\right]\right]+2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}(AP)}_{\mathcal{E}_{A}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})^{2}P]]-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[PS(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})P]+\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[PA(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})P\right]+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}P]}_{\mathcal{E}_{R}}\,.

\mathcal{E}_{D}
,
\mathcal{E}_{A}
and
\mathcal{E}_{R}
are reduced to Gibbs averages of products of overlaps between two replicas. However, for
\mathcal{E}_{A}
, one needs various applications of Stein’s lemma to further the simplification. After this, various cavity estimates help show that the
O(1/n)
scaling terms
exactly
cancel to obtain
\mathcal{E}_{A}+\mathcal{E}_{D}=O_{\beta,q^{*}}(1)
, while various “remainder” terms (higher-order moments and diagonally-weighted cumulants) can be handled via a mixture of cavity estimtates and concentration. The rank-
1
terms make no important contribution as
\mathcal{E}_{R}=O(1)
. The final bound follows from
Corollary
4.17
.

These terms are individually simplified using replica identities for the Gibbs measures leading to various cancellations. The surviving terms reduce to understanding products of overlaps under the Gibbs measure for the planted SK model. Control of the even moments of the overlap deviations for the planted SK model is extracted from
[
7
, §3]
. This allows one to conclude that the deviations around the overlap are sufficiently small, and the net error in the estimation is
O_{\beta,t}(1)
for the “lower order” terms. Arranging for the cancellations of the “higher order” terms requires precise limiting estimates for various mixed moments of the overlaps, which are established by using a modified cavity interpolation for the planted SK model in
§
5.4
. This approach can be seen as a substantial and technically involved generalization of the one adopted in
[
6
, §3]
.

In the following proofs, we will use notions of “rectangular sums” (
f,\widehat{f}
), rescaled-and-recentered spins (
\widehat{\sigma}_{i}
), cubic “bulk” terms such as
\nu_{1}(fG^{-})
where
G^{-}\in\mathcal{Q}
, and the limits for these denoted as
U_{n},V_{n}
and
W_{n}
, as well as various other objects (and the pertinent notation) introduced in
§
5
.

Lemma 4.11
(Controlling the
\mathcal{E}_{D}
term)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
G=G_{A,y_{t},\beta}
be the (tilted/planted) SK Gibbs measure at
t\in[0,T]
, and

m_{t}:=\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle\in(-1,1)^{n},\qquad P:=\mathop{{}\bm{\mathrm{Cov}}}(G),\qquad P_{ij}=\left\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle\sigma_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\sigma_{j}\right\rangle\,.

Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{D}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\left(W^{(2)}_{n}-\frac{4}{n}\right)+\frac{n^{2}}{4}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\left\langle f^{2}\right\rangle\right]+\frac{n^{2}}{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left\langle f\widehat{f}\right\rangle\right]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P]\right]+\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+2\left(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]\right]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P]\right]\right)+\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}+O(1)

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]+3\mathbb{E}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}+O(1)\,.

where
W^{(2)}_{n}
is defined in
§
5.4
and
\mathcal{R}^{D}_{V}
is defined in the proof.

Proof 14
.

Denote
m:=m_{t}=\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle
and define the rescaled and re-centered overlaps as
\widehat{\sigma_{i}}:=\frac{\sigma_{i}-m_{i}}{\sqrt{1-m_{i}^{2}}}
. Observe that

P_{ij}=\left\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle\sigma_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\sigma_{j}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\left(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i}\right)\left(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{2}_{j}\right)\right\rangle\,.

Notate
V:=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}mm^{\mathsf{T}}
and
c=\beta^{2}(1-\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}/n)
. The goal is to separate the terms into those
with
the rank-1 projector
V
and those without them. The former will be shown to be small by using concentration of cubic “bulk” observables established in
Proposition
5.21
and
Lemma
5.22
. The latter will be estimated using replica identities and the CLT estimates developed in
§
5.4
and made to cancel with some terms in
\mathcal{E}_{A}
.

Expanding the trace and using its linearity,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{D}

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PS^{2}P]-2\mathsf{Tr}[SP]+n\right]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P(D+cI_{n}-V)^{2}P]\right]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[(D+cI_{n}-V)P]\right]+n

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PD^{2}P]\right]+2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P(VD+DV)P]]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[PVP]]+\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PV^{2}P]]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[DP]]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[P]]+2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[VP]]+n

\displaystyle=\left(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PD^{2}P]\right]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[Tr[DP]]+n\right)+\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\left(\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]-\mathsf{Tr}[P]\right)]+\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}\,,

where
\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}
contains all tracial terms that have at least one copy of the rank-
1
projector
V
.

Evaluating
\mathsf{Tr}[DP]
.
Note that
P_{ii}=1-m_{i}^{2}
and
D(m)_{ii}=\frac{1}{1-m_{i}^{2}}
, which implies

\mathsf{Tr}\left[DP\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-m_{i}^{2}}(1-m_{i}^{2})=n\,.

Evaluating
\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]
.
Using the fact that
f=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{3}_{i}-\sigma^{4}_{i})
,
\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]=\left\lVert{P}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}
and standard replica identities, one obtains the following algebraic simplification via
Lemma
A.3
, (
A.4
) as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\left\langle f^{2}\right\rangle\,.

Evaluating
\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]
.
By a similar calculation using
Lemma
A.3
, one obtains

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\left\langle f\widehat{f}\right\rangle\,.

Evaluating
\mathsf{Tr}[PD^{2}P]
.
By yet another analogous calculation, this yields the “doubly-scaled” quadratic term,

\mathsf{Tr}[PD^{2}P]=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\left\langle ff^{(2)}\right\rangle\,.

Taking expectation, and setting
W^{(2)}_{n}=\nu_{1}(f\,f^{(2)})
along with an application of
Proposition
5.24
gives the
\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]
in
\mathcal{E}_{D}
. The final step is applying
Lemma
5.30
to the
2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c(\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]-\mathsf{Tr}[P])\right]
term to obtain a factor of
2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]
.

We now dispense with the
\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}
term first, by using a mixture of overlap concentration and control over the moments of certain quadratic bulk observables, showing that it is
O(1)
.

Lemma 4.12
(
\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}=O(1)
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}
be as defined in
Lemma
4.11
. Then,

\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}=O(1)\,.

Proof 15
.

From the proof of
Lemma
4.11
, note that

\mathcal{R}^{V}_{D}=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PV^{2}P]-\mathsf{Tr}[P(VD+DV)P]-2c\mathsf{Tr}[PVP]+2\mathsf{Tr}[VP]\right]\,.

Since
V=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}mm^{\mathsf{T}}
, it is immediate that

\mathsf{Tr}[PVP]=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}m,\quad\mathsf{Tr}[VP]=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}m^{\mathsf{T}}Pm,\quad\mathsf{Tr}[PV^{2}P]=\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}m\,.

Similarly,

\mathsf{Tr}[PDVP]=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}(Dm)=\mathsf{Tr}[PVDP]\,.

The quadratic forms are scaled by powers of
1/n
and
\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}\leqslant n
, so it will suffice to show that

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\langle m,Pm\rangle=O(n),\quad\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\langle m,P^{2}m\rangle=O(n),\quad\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\langle m,P^{2}(Dm)\rangle=O(n)\,.

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[VP]]=O(1)
via overlap-concentration.
This is the easy term, and follows directly from overlap concentration after applying replica identities to rewrite the quadratic form as a straightforward quadratic polynomial of replica overlaps. Observe the following

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[VP]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m^{\mathsf{T}}Pm\right]=\sum_{i,j}P_{i,g}m_{i}m_{j}=\sum_{i,j}\left(\left\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle\sigma_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\sigma_{j}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\sigma_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\sigma_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i,j}\left\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\right\rangle\left\langle\sigma_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\sigma_{j}\right\rangle-\sum_{i,j}\left\langle\sigma_{i}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle\sigma_{j}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}n^{2}\left(\left\langle R_{1,2}R_{1,3}\right\rangle-\left\langle R_{1,2}\right\rangle^{2}\right)=2\beta^{2}n\left(\left\langle(R_{1,2}-q^{*})(R_{1,3}-q^{*})\right\rangle-\left\langle(R_{1,2}-q^{*})\right\rangle^{2}\right)\,.

Taking expectations
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\cdot]
, Cauchy-Schwarz, Jensen, and invoking overlap-concentration yields

\displaystyle 2\beta^{2}n\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\left\langle R_{1,2}R_{1,3}\right\rangle-\left\langle R_{1,2}\right\rangle^{2}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\beta^{2}n\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\left\langle R_{1,2}-q^{*}\right\rangle\right|\left|\left\langle R_{1,3}-q^{*}\right\rangle\right|\right]+2\beta^{2}n\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(R_{1,2}-q^{*})\right\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\beta^{2}n\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(R_{1,2}-q^{*})^{2}\right\rangle\right]}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(R_{1,3}-q^{*})^{2}\right\rangle\right]}+2\beta^{2}n\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(R_{1,2}-q^{*})^{2}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\beta^{2}n\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}+2\beta^{2}\frac{C}{n}=O(1)\,.

This immediately implies that

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[VP]]=O_{\beta}(1)\,.

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PVP]]
and
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PV^{2}P]]
are
O_{\beta}(1)
via
Proposition
5.21
.
We expand the trace and use replica identities to write the term as a product of centered overlaps, weighted by the “rectangular sum”
f
, which can shown to be
O(n^{-2})
by
Proposition
5.21
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[PVP]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}m=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i,j,k}m_{i}P_{ik}P_{kj}m_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{2n}\sum_{i,j,k}\left\langle\sigma^{5}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{2}_{k})\right\rangle\left\langle(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{3}_{k}-\sigma^{4}_{k})\right\rangle\left\langle\sigma^{6}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{2n}\left(\sum_{i}\left\langle\sigma^{5}_{i}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})\right\rangle\right)\left(\sum_{j}\left\langle\sigma^{6}_{j}(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})\right\rangle\right)\left(\sum_{k}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{2}_{k})(\sigma^{3}_{k}-\sigma^{4}_{k})\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}n^{2}}{2}\left\langle(R_{1,5}-R_{2,5})(R_{3,6}-R_{3,4})f\right\rangle=\frac{\beta^{2}n^{2}}{2}\left\langle(\widehat{R}_{1,5}-\widehat{R}_{2,5})(\widehat{R}_{3,6}-\widehat{R}_{3,4})f\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}n^{2}}{2}\left(\left\langle f\widehat{R}_{1,5}\widehat{R}_{3,6}\right\rangle-\left\langle f\widehat{R}_{1,5}\widehat{R}_{3,4}\right\rangle-\left\langle f\widehat{R}_{2,5}\widehat{R}_{3,6}\right\rangle+\left\langle f\widehat{R}_{2,5}\widehat{R}_{3,4}\right\rangle\right)\,.

Now, taking expectations
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\cdot]
and applying
Proposition
5.21
to each of the four terms above yields that

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PVP]\right]=\frac{\beta^{2}n^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{C_{1}}{n^{2}}+\frac{C_{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{C_{3}}{n^{2}}+\frac{C_{4}}{n^{2}}\right)=O_{\beta}(1)\,,

since
\nu_{1}(fG^{-})=Cn^{-2}
for any quadratic bulk observable
G^{-}
such as
\widehat{R}^{-}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\widehat{R}^{-}_{r,r^{\prime}}
(
5.47
), and

\displaystyle\nu(f\widehat{R}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\widehat{R}_{r,r^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\nu(fG^{-})}_{=Cn^{-2}\text{~\eqref{eq:fminus-replace}}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\nu(f(\varepsilon^{\ell}\varepsilon^{\ell^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{r}\varepsilon^{r^{\prime}}))}_{\leqslant C^{\prime}n^{-2}}+\frac{1}{n}\nu(f\varepsilon^{\ell}\varepsilon^{\ell^{\prime}}\widehat{R}^{-}_{r,r^{\prime}})+\frac{1}{n}\nu(f\varepsilon^{r}\varepsilon^{r^{\prime}}\widehat{R}^{-}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{Cauchy-Schwarz}}Cn^{-2}+\frac{2}{n}\sqrt{\nu(f^{2})}\sqrt{\nu((\widehat{R}^{-}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}})^{2})}

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf}}\,+\,overlap-concentration}}Cn^{-2}\,.

Similarly,

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PV^{2}P]]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}\braket{m|P^{2}m}\right]\leqslant_{\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}\leqslant n}\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PVP]\right]\leqslant O_{\beta}(1)\,.

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PDVP]]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PVDP]]=O_{\beta}(1)
via
Proposition
5.21
.
Since
\mathsf{Tr}[PVDP]=\mathsf{Tr}[PDVP]=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}(Dm)
, it suffices to bound the quadratic form
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}(Dm)]=\Theta(n)
. To this end, note that

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}(Dm)\right]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}P^{2}_{i,j}m_{i}D_{jj}m_{j}\right]=\sum_{i,j}m_{i}m_{j}D_{jj}\sum_{k}P_{ik}P_{kj}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{ij}m_{i}m_{j}D_{jj}\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k}\left(\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{2}_{k})\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle(\sigma^{3}_{k}-\sigma^{4}_{k})(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\left(\sum_{i}m_{i}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})\right)\left(\sum_{j}m_{j}D_{jj}(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})\right)\left(\sum_{k}(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{2}_{k})(\sigma^{3}_{k}-\sigma^{4}_{k})\right)\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\left(\sum_{j}m_{j}D_{jj}(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\sigma^{1}_{i}-m^{*}\right)\,f\right\rangle\right]-\frac{n^{2}}{4}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\left(\sum_{j}m_{j}D_{jj}(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\sigma^{2}_{i}-m^{*}\right)\,f\right\rangle\right]\,.

Using the fact that
|m_{j}|\leqslant 1
uniformly for every
j\in[n]
, and noting that the three terms in the summand are either a bulk-deviation (like magnetization deviation), the rectangular sum (
f
), or a diagonally-weighted summand, means a direct invocation of
Lemma
5.27
shows the above is
O(n)
which concludes the bound
6
6

6

See
Remark
5.26
.
.

Below, we bound the
\mathcal{A}
term using a similar approach. The computation and simplifications are longer and more tedious than those involved in controlling the
\mathcal{A}
term, but the changes are not fundamental.

Lemma 4.13
(Controlling the
\mathcal{E}_{A}
term and exact cancellations)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
G=G_{A,y_{t},\beta}
be the Gibbs measure of the planted SK model with tilt
y_{t}
at time
t\in[0,T]
, with

m:=\langle\sigma\rangle\in(-1,1)^{n},\qquad P:=\mathop{{}\bm{\mathrm{Cov}}}(G),\qquad P_{ij}=\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\langle\sigma_{j}\rangle.

Let

q:=\frac{\|m\|_{2}^{2}}{n},\qquad c:=\beta^{2}(1-q)=\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathsf{Tr}[P],\qquad V:=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}mm^{\mathsf{T}},\qquad S:=D+cI_{n}-V.

Then,

(4.6)

\mathcal{E}_{A}=-\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]-3\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+O(1)+\mathcal{R}_{A}^{V}+\mathcal{R}_{A}^{\mathrm{resp}},

where

•

\mathcal{R}_{A}^{V}
is the collection of all contributions containing at least one copy of
V
, and

•

\mathcal{R}_{A}^{\mathrm{resp}}
is the collection of all “response” contributions coming from
Gaussian integration-by-parts in
A
where at least one
A
–derivative hits either
D
,
c
,
or produces a non-diagonal spin-correlation response .

Proof.

The proof follows by applying algebraic simplifications after applications of Stein’s lemma (
Proposition
4.4
) and explicit evaluations of derivatives of the Gibbs density to obtain trace terms that exactly cancel those in
\mathcal{E}_{D}
, while collecting the remaining terms into another remainder
\mathcal{R}^{V}_{A}
which can be shown to be
O(1)
via overlap/magnetization concentration, as well as the cubic and diagonally-weighted remainder estimates in
§
5.4
.

Steins’s lemma-type identities.
Write the SK term in the planted SK model Hamiltonian as

\frac{\beta}{2}\langle\sigma,A\sigma\rangle=\beta\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}A_{ij}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}+\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{ii}\,.

The diagonal piece
\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i}A_{ii}
is
\sigma
-independent, and so
P,m,D,c,V,S
do
not
depend on
\{A_{ii}\}_{i}
(
Lemma
A.1
). All applications of
Proposition
4.4
are performed only over
\{A_{ij}\}_{i<j}
. For
i<j
,
A_{ij}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/n)
, and so using
Proposition
4.4
gives

(4.7)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[A_{ij}\,F(A)\big]=\frac{1}{n}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[\partial_{ij}F(A)\big]\,.

For the quadratic term we apply
Proposition
4.4
in conjunction with the product rule to obtain

(4.8)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[A_{ij}A_{kl}\,F(A)\big]=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)=(k,l)\}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F(A)]+\frac{1}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[A_{kl}\,\partial_{ij}F(A)\big],\qquad(i<j,\;k<l).

Simplifying
\mathcal{E}_{A}
and isolating the rank-
1
part.
Using
S=D+cI-V
and trace cyclicity,

\mathsf{Tr}[PSAP]=\mathsf{Tr}[P(D+cI-V)AP]=\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]+c\,\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]-\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}V]\,.

This immediately gives,

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{A}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\beta^{2}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]-2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]-2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]+2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP]}_{:=\,\mathcal{E}^{(0)}_{A}}+\underbrace{2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}V]}_{:=\,\mathcal{R}_{A}^{V}}.

\mathcal{R}_{A}^{V}
will contain
all
terms that contain at least one copy of
V
after performing the Stein’s lemma based expansions.
We first evaluate the “
V
-free” part, which gives

\mathcal{E}_{A}^{(0)}:=\beta^{2}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]-2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]-2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]+2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP]\,.

Rewriting Gaussian trace terms as sums with symmetric coefficients.
For any
M\in M_{n}(\mathbb{R})
that is independent of the diagonal of
A
,

\mathsf{Tr}[AM]=\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}\big(M_{ij}+M_{ji}\big).

Since
P
is symmetric, so is
P^{2}
, hence
P^{2}_{ij}=P^{2}_{ji}
.
Moreover
P^{2}D
is not symmetric, but

(P^{2}D)_{ij}+(P^{2}D)_{ji}=(P^{2})_{ij}D_{jj}+(P^{2})_{ij}D_{ii}=(P^{2})_{ij}(D_{ii}+D_{jj}).

Thus

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[AP]

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}P_{ij},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij},

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}(D_{ii}+D_{jj}).

(4.13)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}A]=\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}(P^{2}A)_{ij}.

Gibbs differentiation identities.
For
i<j
and any bounded observable
F(\sigma)
,

(4.14)

\partial_{ij}\langle F\rangle=\beta\Big(\langle F\,\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-\langle F\rangle\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle\Big).

In particular, with
m_{k}:=\langle\sigma_{k}\rangle
and
P_{ab}:=\langle\sigma_{a}\sigma_{b}\rangle-m_{a}m_{b}
, applying
Proposition
4.2
gives

(4.15)

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}m_{a}

\displaystyle=\beta\Big(\langle\sigma_{a}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-m_{a}\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle\Big),

(4.16)

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}P_{ab}

\displaystyle=\beta\Big(\langle(\sigma_{a}-m_{a})(\sigma_{b}-m_{b})\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-P_{ab}\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle\Big).

Similarly, using
Proposition
4.2
on
D_{aa}=(1-m_{a}^{2})^{-1}
and
c=\beta^{2}(1-\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}/n)
gives

(4.17)

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}D_{aa}

\displaystyle=\frac{2m_{a}}{(1-m_{a}^{2})^{2}}\,\partial_{ij}m_{a}=2m_{a}D_{aa}^{2}\,\partial_{ij}m_{a}\,,

(4.18)

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}c

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{a=1}^{n}m_{a}\,\partial_{ij}m_{a}.

Apply
Proposition
4.4
on
\mathcal{E}^{(0)}_{A}
.
We apply (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) to (
4.9
),
and we systematically split each resulting derivative into:

(1)

A
trace-polynomial
part, which is obtained by keeping only the contributions in which
the
A
-derivative produces
\partial_{ij}P_{ij}
-type or
\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}
-type
contributions that close on traces,

(2)

A
response remainder
part, which collects every other contribution – those where the derivative hits
D
,
c
, or creates a non-diagonal spin-response such as

\partial_{ij}P_{ik}
with
k\notin\{i,j\}
, or
\partial_{ij}(PAP)_{ij}
through
\partial_{ij}P
with non-matching indices,
or those in which a second IBP leaves a surviving factor of
A
. As we will see, most of the higher-order remainder terms and trace powers that have
O(1)
contribution come from these terms.

We simplify each term individually.

The
2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[AP]]
term.
Using (
4.10
) and (
4.7
),

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP]=2\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[A_{ij}P_{ij}]=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{ij}P_{ij}].

The contribution is obtained by applying (
4.16
) with
a=i
and
b=j
as

\partial_{ij}P_{ij}=\beta\Big(\langle(\sigma_{i}-m_{i})(\sigma_{j}-m_{j})\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-P_{ij}\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle\Big)=\beta\big(P_{ii}P_{jj}-P_{ij}^{2}\big),

where the last equality follows from direct computation. Using the tracial expansion,

(4.19)

2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP]=\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[P_{ii}P_{jj}-P_{ij}^{2}\big]\,.

Note that

\sum_{i<j}P_{ii}P_{jj}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\mathsf{Tr}[P]^{2}-\sum_{i}P_{ii}^{2}\Big),\qquad\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]-\sum_{i}P_{ii}^{2}\Big).

Subtracting and taking
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\cdot]
gives

2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[AP]]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}(P_{ii}P_{jj}-P_{ij}^{2})\right]=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\left(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P]^{2}\right]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]\right)=2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[P]]-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]\,.

The second term will be shown to be
O(1)
in the proof of
Lemma
4.15
.

The
-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left(c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]+\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]\right)
terms.
Similar calculations allow for extraction of the “heavy” components.
Using (
4.12
) and (
4.7
),

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]=\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[A_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\Big]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[\partial_{ij}\big((P^{2})_{ij}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\big)\Big].

Using the product rule and summing yields,

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{ij}\big((P^{2})_{ij}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\big)]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(\partial_{ij}P^{2}_{ij})(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\right]+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}\big(\partial_{ij}D_{ii}+\partial_{ij}D_{jj}\big)\right]}_{:=\,\mathrm{DW}\in\mathcal{R}_{A}^{\mathrm{resp}}}\,.

Writing
P^{2}_{ij}=\sum_{k}P_{ik}P_{kj}
, the main contribution is obtained by keeping only the
k=i
and
k=j
pieces
(which are the only ones in which
\partial_{ij}
produces
\partial_{ij}P_{ij}
rather than a genuinely non-diagonal response).
Specifically,

\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}=\beta\Big(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}+P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}+2P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\Big)+R^{6}_{i,j,ij}\,,

where
R^{6}_{i,j,ij}
once again collects the centered third-moment and fourth cumulant terms (
Lemma
A.2
).
Substituting gives,

(4.20)

\displaystyle-2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}(AP^{2}D)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\Big(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}+P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}+2P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2\beta}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\,R^{6}_{i,j,ij}\Big]\;+\;\mathrm{DW}.

We now rewrite the main sum in traces. Denote

S_{1}:=\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\big(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}+P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}\big).

Expand
S_{1}
into the four relevant pieces as

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i<j}\Big(D_{ii}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}+D_{jj}P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}\Big)+\sum_{i<j}\Big(D_{ii}P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}+D_{jj}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}\Big).

Since
D_{ii}P_{ii}=1
,

\sum_{i<j}\Big(D_{ii}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}+D_{jj}P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}\Big)=\sum_{i<j}\big((P^{2})_{jj}+(P^{2})_{ii}\big)=(n-1)\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}].

For the “ratio” part, using symmetry under swapping
(i,j)
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}\Big(D_{ii}P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}+D_{jj}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\neq j}D_{ii}P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}=\sum_{i}D_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\sum_{j\neq i}P_{jj}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}[P]\sum_{i}D_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}-\sum_{i}D_{ii}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{2}]-\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}].

Using
\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{2}]=\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]
, we get

(4.21)

S_{1}=(n-1)\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]+\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]-\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]=(n-2)\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]+\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[PDP].

Similarly, let

S_{2}:=\sum_{i<j}2(D_{ii}+D_{jj})P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}.

Again by symmetry,

\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}=\sum_{i\neq j}D_{ii}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}.

Noting the following expansion for the cubic part,

\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]=\sum_{i,j}D_{ii}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}=\sum_{i}D_{ii}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}+\sum_{i\neq j}D_{ii}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}=\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]+\sum_{i\neq j}D_{ii}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\,,

gives the following final expression

(4.22)

S_{2}=2\big(\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]-\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\big)=2\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]-2\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}].

Combining (
4.21
) and (
4.22
) yields

(4.23)

S_{1}+S_{2}=(n-4)\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]+\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]+2\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}].

Inserting (
4.23
) into (
4.20
) yields that

(4.24)

\displaystyle-2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[(n-4)\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]+\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]+2\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\Big(-\frac{2\beta}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\,R^{6}_{i,j,ij}\Big]\;+\mathrm{DW}\Big)}_{=:\ \mathcal{R}_{AP^{2}D}^{\mathrm{resp}}}\,.

To control the
-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]]
term, note that

-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]]=-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i}(AP^{2})_{i}\right]=-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}\left((P^{2})_{ij}+(P^{2})_{ji}\right)\right]=-4\beta\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[cA_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]\,.

Applying (
4.7
) gives

-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]]=-\frac{4\beta}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ij}\left(c(P^{2})_{ij}\right)\right]\,.

At this point, the product rule followed by
Lemma
A.2
along with some algebra and tracial identities yields that

-\frac{4\beta}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\,\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]=-4\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]+\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]-R^{(3)}_{1}-R^{(3)}_{2}\,,

where

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left(\sum_{k}P_{kj}\left(m_{i}T_{ikj}+m_{j}T_{iki}+\Gamma_{ikij}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left(\sum_{k}P_{ik}\left(m_{i}T_{kjj}+m_{j}T_{kji}+\Gamma_{kjij}\right)\right)\right]\,,

and

-\frac{4\beta}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}\partial_{ij}c\right]=-\frac{8\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}\right]-R^{(3)}_{3}\,,

where

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{3}=-\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}\sum_{k}\left(m_{i}T_{kkj}m_{j}T_{kki}+\Gamma_{kkij}\right)\right]\,.

Putting these together yields the following final expression

\displaystyle-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]\right]

\displaystyle=-4\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]+\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left(\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right)\right]-\frac{8\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}]\right]

\displaystyle-R^{(3)}_{1}-R^{(3)}_{2}-R^{(3)}_{3}\,.

Simple algebra along with the substitution that
c=\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathsf{Tr}[P]
yields the final expression as

\displaystyle-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left(c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]+\mathsf{Tr}\left[AP^{2}D\right]\right)=-2\beta^{2}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\;-\;2\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\,\mathsf{Tr}(PDP)]-4\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]

\displaystyle\;-\;\underbrace{\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]\;+\;\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\;-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]+\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left(\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right)\right]-\frac{8\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}\right]}_{:=\mathrm{R}_{t0}}

(4.25)

\displaystyle-R^{(1)}-R^{(2)}-R^{(3)}+\mathcal{R}_{AP^{2}D}^{\mathrm{resp}}\,.

All terms, excluding the first three, are shown to be
O(1)
. The last four terms are higher-order, and shown to be
O(1)
in the proof of
Lemma
4.15
, whereas the trace polynomial terms in
\mathrm{R}_{t0}
are also
O(1)
and this is proved at various places in the proof of
Lemma
4.15
(see
Remark
4.14
).

At this point, we invoke
Lemma
5.30
to obtain that

-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]\right]=-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P]\right]-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+O(1)\,.

This yields

-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left(c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]+\mathsf{Tr}\left[AP^{2}D\right]\right)=-2\beta^{2}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}\left[P^{2}\right]-6\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}\left[P^{2}\right]\big]-2\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[c\mathsf{Tr}\left[P\right]\big]+\text{ remainder terms}\,.

The
\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]]
term.
Using similar simplifications for tracial expansions,

\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]\right]=\beta^{2}\sum_{i,j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ij}(P^{2}A)_{ji}\right]=\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}\left((P^{2}A)_{ij}+(P^{2}A)_{ji}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{ii}(P^{2}A)_{ii}\right]\,.

The diagonal piece can be computed using the fact that
\mathop{{}\bm{\mathrm{Var}}}(A_{ii})=2/n
and every entry in
P
is independent of the diagonal of
A
via
Lemma
A.1
. Specifically,

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ii}(P^{2}A)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ii}(P^{2})_{ik}A_{ik}\right]=\sum_{i}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A^{2}_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A^{2}_{ii}\right]\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[(P^{2})_{ii}]=\frac{2}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]\,.

For the first term, we use (
4.7
) along with index algebra to simplify as

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}\left((P^{2}A)_{ij}+(P^{2}A)_{ji}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[\partial_{ij}\big((P^{2}A)_{ji}+(P^{2}A)_{ij}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ii}+(P^{2})_{jj}+\sum_{k}A_{kj}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}+\sum_{k}A_{ki}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jk}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ii}+(P^{2})_{jj}\right]+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}A_{kj}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}+\sum_{k}A_{ki}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jk}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n-1}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}A_{kj}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}+\sum_{k}A_{ki}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jk}\right]\,.

Adding the previous simplification into the above equation yields

\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]\right]=\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}A_{kj}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}+\sum_{k}A_{ki}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jk}\right]+\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]

We will now use
(
A.2
)
and (
4.8
) to simplify the remaining off-diagonal term further into a main term, and one that involves third centered moments and fourth cumulants (which will be part of a remainder term). First, using
(
A.2
)
gives

\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}=\beta(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jk}+P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}+(P^{2})_{ii}P_{kj}+(P^{2})_{ij}P_{ki})+R^{1}_{i,j,ik}\,,

where
R^{1}_{i,j,ik}
is a linear combination of terms involving the centered third-moments and fourth cumulants. Doing the same for the
\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jk}
term and summing over
k
gives

\sum_{k}A_{kj}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}=\beta\sum_{k}A_{kj}\Big(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jk}+P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}+(P^{2})_{ii}P_{kj}+(P^{2})_{ij}P_{ki}\Big)+\sum_{k}R^{1}_{i,j,ik}\,.

Collapsing the sums over
k
for each summand gives

\sum_{k}A_{kj}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}=\beta\left(P_{ii}(AP^{2})_{jj}+P_{ij}(AP^{2})_{ji}+(P^{2})_{ii}(AP)_{jj}+(P^{2})_{ij}(AP)_{ji}\right)+\sum_{k}R^{1}_{i,j,kj}\,.

A similar calculation gives

\sum_{k}A_{ki}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jk}=\beta\left(P_{jj}(AP^{2})_{ii}+P_{ij}(AP^{2})_{ij}+(P^{2})_{jj}(AP)_{ii}+(P^{2})_{ij}(AP)_{ij}\right)+\sum_{k}R^{2}_{i,j,jk}\,.

Note the following trace identities

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}\Big(P_{ii}(AP^{2})_{jj}+P_{jj}(AP^{2})_{ii}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]-\sum_{i}P_{ii}(AP^{2})_{ii},

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}\big((AP^{2})_{ji}+(AP^{2})_{ij}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}[PAP^{2}]-\sum_{i}P_{ii}(AP^{2})_{ii},

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}\Big((P^{2})_{ii}(AP)_{jj}+(P^{2})_{jj}(AP)_{ii}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\mathsf{Tr}[AP]-\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}(AP)_{ii},

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}\big((AP)_{ji}+(AP)_{ij}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}AP]-\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}(AP)_{ii}\,,

which we substitute into the in the above two terms and sum over
i<j
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}A_{kj}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ik}+\sum_{k}A_{ki}\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jk}\right]=

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]+\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\mathsf{Tr}[AP]\right]+\underbrace{\left(\frac{\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[2\mathsf{Tr}(PAP^{2})-2\sum_{i}P_{ii}(AP^{2})_{ii}-2\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}(AP)_{ii}\right]\right)}_{:=\mathcal{R}^{lo}_{PA^{2}P}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\sum_{k}R^{1}_{i,j,kj}+\sum_{k}R^{2}_{i,j,jk}\right)\right]}_{:=\mathcal{R}^{ho}_{PA^{2}P}}\,.

We now apply
(
4.7
)
to the first two terms in the expansion above (dropping diagonal terms, as
P
is independent of the diagonal of
A)
and collect the remaining three as remainder terms in
\mathcal{R}^{AP}
. For the first term, using the product rule, (
4.7
), and
Lemma
A.2
immediately gives

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]]=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{u<v}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[\partial_{uv}\big(\mathsf{Tr}[P]\,(P^{2})_{uv}\big)\big]=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{u<v}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[(\partial_{uv}\mathsf{Tr}[P])(P^{2})_{uv}+\mathsf{Tr}[P]\,\partial_{uv}(P^{2})_{uv}\big]

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:dP-pairing]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:dP-pairing}}}}\frac{2\beta}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P]^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\underbrace{\frac{2\beta}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[Tr[P]\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]+\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]-2\mathsf{Tr}[P]\sum_{u}P_{uu}(P^{2})_{uu}-\sum_{u}(P^{2}_{uu})^{2}\right]}_{:=\mathrm{R}_{t1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{2\beta}{n}\sum_{u<v}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{uv}\left(m_{u}\sum_{a}T_{aav}+m_{v}\sum_{a}T_{aau}+\sum_{a}\Gamma_{aauv}\right)\right]}_{:=\mathrm{A}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{2}{n}\sum_{u<v}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P]\left(\sum_{a}P_{av}(m_{u}T_{uav}+m_{v}T_{uau}+\Gamma_{uauv})+\sum_{a}P_{ua}(m_{u}T_{avv}++\Gamma_{avuv})\right)\right]}_{:=\mathrm{B}}\,.

This immediately gives

\frac{\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]]=2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]+\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathrm{R}_{t1}+\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathrm{A}+\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathrm{B}\,,

where
\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\left(\mathrm{A}+\mathrm{B}\right)
are of exactly the form of the terms
R^{(3)}_{1},R^{(3)}_{2},R^{(3)}_{3}
defined in the proof of
Lemma
4.15
, where they are shown to be
O(1)
. For
\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathrm{R}_{t1}
, refer to
Remark
4.14
.

Similar calculations as the above also yield that

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\mathsf{Tr}[AP]\right]=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{u<v}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{uv}(\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]P_{uv})\right]=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{u<v}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P]\partial_{uv}P_{uv}+P_{uv}\partial_{uv}\mathsf{Tr}[P]\right]

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:dP-pairing]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:dP-pairing}}}}\frac{\beta}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\mathsf{Tr}[P]^{2}\right]-\underbrace{\frac{\beta}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]^{2}]\right]+\frac{4\beta}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]-\frac{4\beta}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{u}P_{uu}(P^{3})_{uu}\right]}_{:=\mathrm{R}_{t2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\underbrace{\frac{4\beta}{n}\sum_{u<v}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{uv}\sum_{a,b}P_{a,b}\left(m_{u}T_{abv}+m_{v}T_{abu}+\Gamma_{abuv}\right)\right]}_{:=\mathrm{C}}\,.

This, again, immediately yields that

\frac{\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\mathsf{Tr}[AP]\right]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\frac{C(\beta}{n}\mathrm{R}_{t2}+\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathrm{C}\,,

where
\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathrm{C}
has the same form as
\mathrm{HO}_{9}+\mathrm{HO}_{10}+\mathrm{HO}_{11}
(defined in
Lemma
4.15
) and is
O(1)
via the bounds proved on the latter in the proof of
Lemma
4.15
, and
\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathrm{R}_{t2}=O(1)
(see
Remark
4.14
).

Combining the above bounds gives the final expression and using the fact that
\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]=O(1)
as shown in (
4.2
) gives

\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]\right]=3\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\beta^{2}E[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]+\mathcal{R}^{lo}_{PA^{2}P}+\mathcal{R}^{ho}_{PA^{2}P}+O(1)\,,

and every type of remainder term in
\mathcal{R}^{lo}_{PA^{2}P}
and
\mathcal{R}^{ho}_{PA^{2}P}
are captured in the set of remainders explicitly defined in the proof of
Lemma
4.15
.

Bounding the

Final bound for

.

) and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{A}

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]-2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]-2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]+2\beta\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP]+2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}V]

\displaystyle\left(-2\beta^{2}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]-6\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[c^{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\big]-2\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[c\mathsf{Tr}(P)\big]+\text{ remainder terms}\right)+2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[P]]+O(1)

Remark 4.14
.

Note that the trace polynomial terms in
\mathrm{R}_{t0}
are explicitly bounded to be
O(1)
in the proof of
Lemma
4.15
as they appear in the explicit “types” of remainders associated to each term in
\mathcal{E}_{A}
.
\mathrm{R}_{t1}
and
\mathrm{R}_{t2}
can be bounded to be
O(1)
by using the bounds
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]=O(n)
,
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]]=O(n)
and
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]]=O(n^{2})
provided in (
4.2
), (
4.2
) and (
4.2
) in conjunction with the pointwise estimates that
0\leqslant P_{ii}\leqslant 1
and
|m_{i}|\leqslant 1
. While this happens at many points during the (lengthy) proof of
Lemma
4.15
, we collect some of these for clarity over here.

\mathrm{R}_{t1}
terms.
Note the following bounds

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\mathsf{Tr}[P]\leqslant n,\,P\succeq 0}\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]\leqslant_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p3-bound}}}O(1)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[eq:c-p4-bound]{{(\ref*{eq:c-p4-bound})}}}}O(1)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P]\sum_{u}P_{uu}(P^{2})_{uu}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\mathsf{Tr}[P]\leqslant n,\,|P_{uu}|\leqslant 1}\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{u}(P^{2})_{uu}\right]=_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p2-bound}}}O(1)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{u}(P^{2}_{uu})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{(P^{2})_{uu}=\sum_{v}(P_{uv})^{2}\leqslant Cn}\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]=_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p2-bound}}}O(1)\,.

\mathrm{R}_{t2}
terms.
Similarly, note the following set of complementary bounds

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=C(\beta)n^{2}\nu(f^{2}_{1234}f^{2}_{5678})=_{\text{CS\,+\,\hyperref@@ii[lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf}}}}O(1)\,

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]

\displaystyle=_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p4-bound}}}O(1)\,

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{u}P_{uu}(P^{3})_{uu}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{0\leqslant P_{uu}\leqslant 1,P\succeq 0}\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]=_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p3-bound}}}O(1)\,.

Lemma 4.15
(Bounding the remainder terms in
\mathcal{R}^{A}
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

The remainder terms in
\mathcal{E}_{A}
are
O(1)
.

Proof.

It is easily verified that the following are the types of remainders contributed by each of the 4 terms in
\mathcal{E}_{A}
7
7

7

Note that, for instance, there are more remainder terms in
\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]]
that come from centered third moments or fourth cumulants after applications of
Lemma
A.2
in the proof of
Lemma
4.13
, but they all have the same “type” with centered third moments with distinct indices or one repeated, and the same for the fourth cumulant terms. By symmmetry, therefore, it suffices to bound a single term of each type as this only loses
O(1)
factors in the bound.

\displaystyle 2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[AP]]

\displaystyle\to\left\{-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right\}\,,

\displaystyle-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}D]]

\displaystyle\to\left\{\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}],\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}],R_{1},R_{2}\right\}\,,

\displaystyle-2\beta\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[c\mathsf{Tr}[AP^{2}]]

\displaystyle\to\left\{\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right],\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left(\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right)\right],\frac{8\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}\right],R^{(3)}_{1},R^{(3)}_{2},R^{(3)}_{3}\right\}\,,

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]]

\displaystyle\to\Bigg\{\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k}A_{ki}m_{i}\sum_{a}P_{ja}T_{akj}\right],\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k}A_{ki}m_{i}\sum_{b}P_{bk}T_{jbj}\right],\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k}A_{ki}\sum_{b}P_{bk}\Gamma_{jbij}\right],

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k}A_{ki}\sum_{a}P_{ja}\Gamma_{akij}\right],\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PAP^{2}]\right],\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}P_{ii}(AP^{2})_{ii}\right],\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}(AP)_{ii}\right]\Bigg\}\,,

where the the remainders containing centered third moments and fourth cumulants, and powers of diagonally weighted overlap-like terms, are specifically

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}\left(\partial_{ij}D_{ii}+\partial_{ij}D_{jj}\right)\right]

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}P_{kj}\left(m_{i}T_{ikj}+m_{j}T_{iki}++\Gamma_{ikij}\right)+\sum_{k}P_{ik}(m_{i}T_{kjj}+m_{j}T_{kji}+\Gamma_{kjij})\right)\right]\,,

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left(\sum_{k}P_{kj}\left(m_{i}T_{ikj}+m_{j}T_{iki}+\Gamma_{ikij}\right)\right)\right]\,,

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left(\sum_{k}P_{ik}\left(m_{i}T_{kjj}+m_{j}T_{kji}+\Gamma_{kjij}\right)\right)\right]\,,

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}\sum_{k}\left(m_{i}T_{kkj}m_{j}T_{kki}+\Gamma_{kkij}\right)\right]\,.

where
T_{abc}
and
\Gamma_{abcd}
are as defined in
Lemma
A.2
.

All of these remainder terms can be rewritten into linear combinations of cubic moments of rectangular sums
f
, weighted by quadratic terms in the overlap/magnetization, or can directly be rewritten in terms of third and fourth moments of rectangular sums that are diagonally weighted. Consequently, judicious applications of
Proposition
5.20
,
Proposition
5.21
and
Lemma
5.27
suffice to show that the remainder terms are
O(1)
.

The tracial terms.
The easiest terms to bound are the tracial terms. Note by
(
A.4
)

\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]=C(\beta)\,\nu(f^{2})\,n\leqslant_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[eq:replicon-2k-moment]{{(\ref*{eq:replicon-2k-moment})}} with }k=1}C(\beta)\,.

Similarly, using the replica identities that
P_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{2}_{j})\right\rangle
and
(P^{2})_{ij}=\frac{n}{4}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})f_{1234}\right\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[PDP^{2}]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}P_{ij}D_{jj}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\langle(\sigma^{5}_{i}-\sigma^{6}_{i})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})\right\rangle\right)D_{jj}\left(\frac{n}{4}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})f_{1234}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{8}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}D_{jj}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{i}-\sigma^{6}_{i})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{8}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left\langle(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})f_{1256}f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{8}\Bigg(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left\langle(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})(R_{15}-q^{*})f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]+\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left\langle(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})(R_{26}-q^{*})f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left\langle(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})(R_{16}-q^{*})f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left\langle(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})(R_{25}-q^{*})f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]\Bigg)\,,

where the last step uses the identity that
