f_{1256}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}a^{1256}_{i}=(R_{15}-q^{*})+(R_{26}-q^{*})-(R_{16}-q^{*})-(R_{25}-q^{*})
. At this point, observe that each of the four terms in a form compliant with
Lemma
5.27
with the addition of one more
(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})
term, which is absolutely bounded by
2
. Invoking the lemma yields that

\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]=O(1)\,.

Using the fact that
c=\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathsf{Tr}[P]=\beta^{2}\left\langle 1-R_{78}\right\rangle
and
\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]=\frac{n^{3}}{8}\left\langle f_{1234}f_{1256}f_{3456}\right\rangle
one obtains that

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]

\displaystyle=C(\beta)n^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle f_{1234}f_{1256}f_{3456}\right\rangle\right]-C(\beta)n^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle R_{78}f_{1234}f_{1256}f_{3456}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{(4,4,4,4)-\text{H\"{o}lder's,~\hyperref@@ii[lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf}}}}C(\beta)n^{2}\,\mathsf{span}\left\{f_{1234}\,\Xi_{ab}\,\Xi_{cd}\right\}+O(1)

(4.26)

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[prop:D15-full]{Proposition~\ref*{prop:D15-full}}}}O(1)\,,

where one uses the fact that
\nu(f\Xi_{1}\Xi_{2})=\nu(f\Xi^{-}_{1}\Xi^{-}_{2})+O(4)
. Similarly, a crude bound using the fact that
P_{ii}\leqslant 1
and
c=\beta^{2}(1-\left\langle R_{12}\right\rangle)\leqslant 2\beta^{2}
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\left(\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]

(4.27)

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[eq:TrP2-f2]{{(\ref*{eq:TrP2-f2})}}}}C^{\prime}(\beta)n\nu(f^{2})=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf}}}}O(1)\,.

Using simple tracial algebra yields that
\sum_{i,j}(P^{2})_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}=\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]
and that
\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}P^{2}_{ji}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]
, which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}\right]\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(\beta)n^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle f_{1234}f_{5678}f_{1278}f_{3456}\right\rangle\right]\leqslant_{(4,4,4,4)\text{-H\"{o}lder's}}C^{\prime\prime}(\beta)n^{2}\nu(f^{4}_{1234})^{1/4}\nu(f^{4}_{5678})^{1/4}\nu(f^{4}_{1278})^{1/4}\nu(f^{4}_{3456})^{1/4}

(4.28)

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf}}}}O(1)\,.

We now bound the tracial terms that involve a factor of
A
and involve an application of
(
4.7
)
before simplification with replica rewrites. Note using
Lemma
A.1
and some standard algebra that

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PAP^{2}]\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ij}P^{3}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k,\ell}(\partial_{ij}P_{ik})P_{k\ell}P_{\ell j}+\sum_{k}P_{ik}(\partial_{ij}P_{k\ell})P_{\ell j}+\sum_{k}P_{ik}P_{k\ell}(\partial_{ij}P_{\ell j})\right]

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:dP-pairing]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:dP-pairing}}}}\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P_{ii}(P^{3})_{jj}+P_{jj}(P^{3})_{ii})\right]+\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[2\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}(P^{3})_{ij}\right]+\mathrm{HO}^{1}+\mathrm{HO}^{2}+\mathrm{HO}^{3}\,,

where each term in
\mathrm{HO}_{i}
is one of five types, namely

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}T_{ikj}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}m_{j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}T_{iki}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}\Gamma_{ikij}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k,\ell}P_{ik}P_{\ell j}T_{k\ell j}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{5}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k,\ell}P_{ik}P_{\ell j}\Gamma_{k\ell ij}\right]\,.

We will show, in the next part of the proof (where we bound the higher-order remainder terms involving centered third-moments and fourth cumulants) that each of these is
O(1)
. So, to obtain the final bound for
\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PAP^{2}]\right]
, we now bound the second-order parts. Simplifying further into trace terms and using the fact that
c=\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathsf{Tr}[P]

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P_{ii}(P^{3})_{jj}+P_{jj}(P^{3})_{ii})\right]+\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[2\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}(P^{3})_{ij}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]+\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]-\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\right]\,.

Note the first two terms are easily shown to be
O(1)
by (
4.2
) and (
4.2
). The final term can be handled by the observation that
\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]=\frac{n^{3}}{8}\nu(f_{1234}f_{3456}f_{1256})
and
0\preceq E_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}[P]\leqslant I_{n}
and
0\preceq P
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\right]\leqslant\frac{4\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]]=C(\beta)n\nu(f_{1234}f_{3456}f_{1256})\leqslant O(n^{-1/2})\,,

by an application of
(2,4,4)
-Hölder’s followed by an invocation of
Lemma
5.16
. This implies that
\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PAP^{2}]\right]=O(1)
.

We turn to the remaining two trace terms from
\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]]
. Note that using the fact that
|\partial_{ij}P_{ii}|\leqslant 2\beta|(m^{2}_{i}P_{ij}-m_{i}m_{j}P_{ii})|\leqslant 8\beta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}P_{ii}(AP^{2})_{ii}\right]=\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ij}\left((P^{2})_{ij}\left(P_{ii}+P_{jj}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left((P^{2})_{ij}\left(\partial_{ij}P_{ii}+\partial_{ij}P_{jj}\right)+\left(P_{ii}+P_{jj}\right)\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:dP-pairing]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:dP-pairing}}\,+\,symmetry}}\frac{32\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]+\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P_{ii}+P_{jj})\left(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}+P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}\right)+2P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathrm{HO}_{6}+\mathrm{HO}_{7}+\mathrm{HO}_{8}\,.

At this point observe that because
P\succeq 0
, a Cauchy–Schwarz impliciation means that

\frac{32\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]\leqslant\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\sqrt{\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]}=O(1)\,,

using (
4.2
). The other pairing term is handled similarly using the facts that
(P_{ii}+P_{jj})(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj})\leqslant 2(P^{2})_{jj}
and analogously on swapping the indices
i
and
j
for the pairing term. This gives

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P_{ii}+P_{jj})\left(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}+P_{jj}(P^{2})_{ii}\right)+2P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[2P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p2-bound},\,CS}}O(1)+\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]]}\leqslant_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p2-bound},\,\eqref{eq:c-p4-bound}}}O(1)+O(n^{-1/2})=O(1)\,.

The higher-order terms have the following types,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})m_{i}\sum_{k}P_{kj}T_{ikj}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{7}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})m_{j}\sum_{k}P_{kj}T_{iki}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{8}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})\sum_{k}P_{kj}\Gamma_{ikij}\right]\,.

These higher-order terms are bounded in the next part of the proof and shown to be
O(1)
. This implies that
\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}P_{ii}(AP^{2})_{ii}\right]=O(1)
.
For the second tracial term, observe that

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}(AP)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ij}P_{ij}\left((P^{2})_{ii}+(P^{2})_{jj}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=_{\eqref{eq:GOE-ibp}}\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(\partial_{ij}P_{ij})\left((P^{2})_{ii}+(P^{2})_{jj}\right)\right]+\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ij}\left(\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ii}+\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jj}\right)\right]\,.

Using the fact that
\partial_{ij}P_{ij}=\beta(P_{ii}P_{jj}-P^{2}_{ij})
and that
|(P_{ii}P_{jj}-P^{2}_{ij})|\leqslant 4
, the first term can be simplified as

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\left|\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(\partial_{ij}P_{ij})\left((P^{2})_{ii}+(P^{2})_{jj}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n^{2}}\left|\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}\left((P^{2})_{ii}+(P^{2})_{jj}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)(n-1)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]=C^{\prime}(\beta)(n-1)\nu(f^{2})=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf}}}}O(1)\,.

To bound the second term, we use similar simplifications to obtain

\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ij}\left(\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{ii}+\partial_{ij}(P^{2})_{jj}\right)\right]=\frac{4\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ik}\partial_{ij}P_{ik}\right]+\frac{4\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{jk}\partial_{ij}P_{jk}\right]\,,

and so, by symmetry in the indices under a
i\leftrightarrow j
swap, it suffices to bound one term. So, note that using
Lemma
A.2
we get that

\displaystyle\frac{4\beta^{3}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ik}\partial_{ij}P_{ik}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ii}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]+\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ij})^{2}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]+\mathrm{HO}_{6}+\mathrm{HO}_{7}+\mathrm{HO}_{8}\,,

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{9}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{i}P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ik}T_{ikj}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{10}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{j}P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ik}T_{iki}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ik}\Gamma_{ikij}\right]\,.

For the lower-order terms, observe that

\displaystyle\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ii}(P_{ij})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}(P^{2}_{ii})\sum_{j}P_{ij}P_{ji}\right]=\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}(P^{2})^{2}_{ii}\right]\leqslant\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{c\leqslant\beta^{2}}\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]]\leqslant C^{\prime}(\beta)n\nu(f^{2})\leqslant_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf]{Lemma~\ref*{lem:replicon-moments-from-mgf}}}}O(1)\,,

and

\displaystyle\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(P_{ij})^{2}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]\leqslant_{P=P^{\mathsf{T}}}\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]\leqslant C(\beta)n^{2}\nu(f_{1234}f_{5678}f_{1278}f_{3456})=O(1)\,,

by (
4.2
). The higher-order terms are, again, shown to be
O(1)
in the next part of the proof. Those bounds complete the proof and show that
\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}(AP)_{ii}\right]=O(1)
.

The derivative terms.
We now use the facts that
\partial_{ij}D_{ij}=2\beta D^{2}_{jj}m_{j}(m_{i}-m_{j}\left\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\right\rangle)
and
m_{i}-m_{j}\left\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\right\rangle=P_{jj}m_{i}-m_{j}P_{ij}
in conjunction with replica identities and symmetrization arguments to bound terms such as
R_{1}
. Some elementary algebra immediately gives

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{j}D^{2}_{jj}\sum_{i\neq j}(P^{2})_{ij}\left(P_{jj}m_{i}-m_{j}P_{ij}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{j}P_{jj}D_{jj}^{2}\sum_{i\neq j}m_{i}(P^{2})_{ij}\Bigg]+\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{j}^{2}D_{jj}^{2}\sum_{i\neq j}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\Bigg].

We collapse the inner sums with replica identities for
P_{ij}
and
(P^{2})_{ij}
using the facts that

\sum_{i\neq j}\Delta\sigma^{k_{1}k_{2}}_{i}\Delta\sigma^{k_{3}k_{4}}_{i}=nf_{k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}\,,

and

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta\sigma^{k_{1}k_{2}}_{i}\sigma^{k_{3}}_{i}=R_{k_{1}k_{3}}-R_{k_{2}k_{3}}\,,

to simplify the term further as

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\,\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{j}P_{jj}D_{jj}^{2}\frac{n^{2}}{4}\Big\langle(R_{15}-R_{25})\,\Delta\sigma_{j}^{34}\,f_{1234}\Big\rangle\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\,\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{j}^{2}D_{jj}^{2}\cdot\frac{n^{2}}{8}\Big\langle\Delta\sigma_{j}^{56}\Delta\sigma_{j}^{34}\,f_{1234}\,f_{1256}\Big\rangle\Bigg]

\displaystyle=-\beta^{2}n\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left(m_{j}(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})\right)((R_{15}-q^{*})-(R_{25}-q^{*}))f_{1234}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\beta^{2}n}{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D^{2}_{jj}\left(m^{2}_{j}(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})\left((R_{15}-q^{*})+(R_{26}-q^{*})-(R_{16}-q^{*})\right)f_{1234}\right)\right]

The first term is a finite linear combination of terms of the form
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\left\langle\sum_{j}D_{jj}a_{n}B_{j}\Xi f_{1234}\right\rangle]
where
B_{j}
is an absolutely bounded function of the spin
j
– this term is bounded, therefore, by a direction application of
Lemma
5.27
. Note that the final term is a linear combination of terms of the form
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\left\langle\sum_{j}D^{2}_{jj}a_{n}B_{j}\Xi f_{1234}\right\rangle]
where
B_{j}
is an absolutely bounded term in the
j
-th spin, and
\Xi
is a centered bulk deviation. Similar to the bound for
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]]
, one can invoke
Lemma
5.27
to bound this
8
8

8

By
Remark
5.26
, note that the extra power of
D_{jj}
does not change the scaling of the bound, since
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\left\langle D^{k}_{jj}\right\rangle]<\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
by
Lemma
5.3
, and nor does any extra factor
B
provided the
a_{n}
is present in the term.
.

The third-centered moment and fourth-cumulant terms.
To control these terms we can use lemmata that bound quartic moments of rectangular sums that are weighted by diagonals and/or bulk deviation terms. To do this, we will use the fact that
(\sigma_{j}-m_{j})^{2}=P_{jj}-2m_{j}(\sigma_{j}-m_{j})
which can be used to show the following simplifications for centered-third moment terms and fourth-cumulant terms when they have repeated indices

T_{kjj}=-2m_{j}P_{kj},\qquad T_{iki}=-2m_{i}P_{ik},\qquad T_{kkj}=-2m_{k}P_{kj},\qquad T_{kki}=-2m_{k}P_{ki}\,,

and

\Gamma_{ikij}=-2P_{ik}P_{ij}-2m_{i}T_{ikj},\qquad\Gamma_{kjij}=-2P_{kj}P_{ij}-2m_{j}T_{kij},\qquad\Gamma_{kkij}=-2P_{ki}P_{kj}-2m_{k}T_{kij}\,.

Using these we bound each term chronologically.

\displaystyle R_{2}=-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}P_{kj}\left(m_{i}T_{ikj}+m_{j}T_{iki}++\Gamma_{ikij}\right)+\sum_{k}P_{ik}(m_{i}T_{kjj}+m_{j}T_{kji}+\Gamma_{kjij})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}P_{kj}(-m_{i}T_{ikj}-2m_{i}m_{j}P_{ik}-2P_{ik}P_{ij})+P_{ik}(-m_{j}T_{kji}-2m_{i}m_{j}P_{kj}-2P_{kj}P_{ij})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(m_{i}m_{j}(P^{2})_{ij}+P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right)\right]+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}(T_{ikj}m_{i}P_{kj}+T_{jki}m_{j}P_{ik})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i\neq j}D_{ii}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]+\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i\neq j}D_{ii}m_{i}m_{j}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}(T_{ikj}m_{i}P_{kj}+T_{jki}m_{j}P_{ik})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{3}]-\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]+\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle m,P^{2}(Dm)\rangle+O_{\beta}\left(\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{2}]\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}(T_{ikj}m_{i}P_{kj}+T_{jki}m_{j}P_{ik})\right)\right]\,.

We have already established earlier in the proof that the first two terms in the equality are
O(1)
. For the third term, note that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle m,P^{2}(Dm)\rangle\right]=O(1)
by the bound for the
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PDVP]]
term in the proof of
Lemma
4.12
. To bound the fourth term, note that

\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[DP^{2}]\right]=\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}D_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]=C(\beta)\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}\left\langle D_{ii}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{3}_{i}-\sigma^{4}_{i})f\right\rangle\right]=C(\beta)n\nu(f\widehat{f})\leqslant_{\text{CS}}O(1)\,,

where we use
Lemma
5.16
and
Lemma
5.17
. To bound the final term, note the following simplifications based on replica rewrites,

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}(T_{ikj}m_{i}P_{kj}+T_{jki}m_{j}P_{ik})\right)\right]=_{T_{ikj}=\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{3}_{k})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\sum_{k}\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{i}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{3}_{k})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{k}-\sigma^{6}_{k})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\sum_{k}\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{j}(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{2}_{k})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{3}_{j})(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{4}_{i})(\sigma^{5}_{i}-\sigma^{6}_{i})(\sigma^{5}_{k}-\sigma^{6}_{k})\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{i}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})f_{1356}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})\left(\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{j}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{4}_{i})(\sigma^{5}_{i}-\sigma^{6}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{3}_{j})f_{1256}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})f_{1356}\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma^{7}_{i}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})\right\rangle\right]+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}D_{ii}\left\langle(\sigma^{7}_{i}f_{1356}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})\right\rangle\sum_{j\neq i}(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=\frac{\beta^{2}n}{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})f_{1256}\left((R^{-j}_{17}-q^{*})-(R^{-j}_{27}-q^{*})\right)\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{\beta^{2}n}{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j}D_{jj}\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{i}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})f_{1256}\left((R^{-i}_{15}-q^{*})+(R^{-i}_{46}-q^{*})-(R^{-i}_{16}-q^{*})-(R^{-i}_{45}-q^{*})\right)\right\rangle\right]\,.

The two terms above are both finite linear combinations of terms of the form
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}D_{ii}\left\langle(\sigma^{a}_{i}-\sigma^{b}_{i})B_{s}f_{abcd}\Xi^{-}\right\rangle\right]
which are easily shown to be
O(1/n)
by
Lemma
5.27
and
Remark
5.26
. This immediately implies, in conjunction with the prior bounds, that

R_{2}=O(1)\,.

Using the same simplifications for the repeated-indices centered third-moments and fourth cumulants mentioned above, it is not hard to show that

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i<j}m_{i}m_{j}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]+\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]+\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k}P_{kj}T_{ikj}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{|c|\leqslant\beta^{2}}\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\,\langle m,P^{2}m\rangle\right]+\frac{8\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]-c\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]+\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k}P_{kj}T_{ikj}\right]\,.

For the first term, note that the fact that
P\succeq 0
and an application of
(1,\infty)
-Hölder’s in conjunction with the fact that
|c|\leqslant\beta^{2}
immediately implies that

\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\,\langle m,P^{2}m\rangle\right]\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle m,P^{2}m\right]\leqslant O(1)\,,

where the final inequality uses the bound for
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PVP]]
in the proof of
Lemma
4.12
. The second term is already bounded by
O(1)
by the bounds developed for the tracial terms earlier in the proof. For the final term, note using replica identities that

\displaystyle\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k}P_{kj}T_{ikj}\right]=\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\left\langle\sum_{k}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{3}_{k})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{k}-\sigma^{6}_{k})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=2\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[c\sum_{i<j}\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{i}(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})f_{1356}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle((1-q^{*})-(R_{8,9}-q^{*}))\left(\sum_{i}(\sigma^{7}_{i}\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{7}_{i}\sigma^{2}_{i})\right)\left(\sum_{j}(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})\right)f_{1356}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=C(\beta,q^{*})n^{2}\mathsf{span}\left\{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(R_{8,9}-q^{*})\Xi_{ab}\Xi_{cd}f_{1356}\right\rangle\right],\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\Xi_{ab}\Xi_{cd}f_{1356}\right\rangle\right]\right\}\,,

where the first set of terms are directly controlled to be
O(n^{-2})
by a
(4,4,4,4)
-Hölder’s inequality, followed by an invocation of
Lemma
5.16
and
Theorem
5.1
. The second set of terms are
O(n^{-2})
by a direct invocation of
Proposition
5.21
.

Due to the symmetry and exact same structure of the terms, an exactly analogous argument shows that

R^{(3)}_{2}=O(1)\,.

We now bound the
R^{(3)}_{3}
term by first simplifying using the same replica identities and repeated-index centered third moment and fourth cumulant simplifications to obtain that

m_{i}T_{kkj}m_{j}T_{kki}=m_{i}(-2m_{k}P_{kj})m_{j}(-2m_{k}P_{ki})=4m_{i}m_{j}m^{2}_{k}P_{jk}P_{ki}\,,

and

\Gamma_{kkij}=-2m_{k}T_{kij}-2P_{ik}P_{kj}\,.

This gives,

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{3}=-\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}\sum_{k}\left(m_{i}T_{kkj}m_{j}T_{kki}+\Gamma_{kkij}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{16\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}m_{i}m_{j}(P\mathsf{diag}(m)P)_{ij}\right]-\frac{8\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}\sum_{k}m_{k}T_{kij}\right]-\frac{8\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{CS}}\frac{16\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sqrt{\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})^{2}_{ij}}\sqrt{\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})^{2}_{ij}}\left\lVert\diag(m^{2})\right\rVert_{\mathsf{op}}\right]-\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]+\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}(P^{2})^{2}_{ii}\right]-\frac{8\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}\sum_{k}m_{k}T_{kij}\right]\,.

For the first three terms, note that
\left\lVert\diag(m^{2})\right\rVert_{\mathsf{op}}\leqslant 1
and that
\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})^{2}_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]-\sum_{i}(P^{2})^{2}_{ii}
. Since
\sum_{i}(P^{2})^{2}_{ii}\leqslant\left\lVert{P^{2}}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}=\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]
, this implies that

\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}(P^{2})^{2}_{ii}\right]\leqslant\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]]=C(\beta)n^{2}\nu(f_{1234}f_{34556}f_{5678}f_{7812})=O(1)\,.

where we use
Lemma
5.16
along with
(4,4,4,4)
-Hölder’s. This immediately bounds the first three terms by
O(1)
. For the final term, observe that

\displaystyle\frac{8\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ij}\sum_{k}m_{k}T_{kij}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i})(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{2}_{j})f_{1234}\right\rangle\sum_{k}\left\langle\sigma^{9}_{k}(\sigma^{5}_{k}-\sigma^{6}_{k})(\sigma^{5}_{i}-\sigma^{7}_{i})(\sigma^{5}_{j}-\sigma^{8}_{j})\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{span}\left\{C(\beta)n^{2}\nu\left(\Xi_{ab}\,\Xi_{cd}\,\Xi_{ef}f_{1234}\right),C^{\prime}(\beta)n\nu\left(f_{1234}\Xi_{ab}F\right)\right\}\,,

where the last equality follows after symmetrizing the summations over
i
and
j
and then summing then
a^{abcd}
factors separately with their index, and
F
is some absolutely bounded function of all replicas.
At this point, note that
(4,4,4,4)
-Hölder’s along with
Theorem
5.1
and
Lemma
5.16
yields the first type of term is
O(1)
, and Cauchy-Schwarz gives
O(1)
bounds for the second type of term.

We now bound the four type of higher-order remainder terms from the
\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]]
term. Note by the repeated index identities, that

\displaystyle\sum_{b}P_{bk}T_{jbj}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{k}-\sigma^{2}_{k})(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{4}_{j})(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})f_{1235}\right\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{a}P_{ja}\Gamma_{akij}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{2}_{j})(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{7}_{j})(\sigma^{3}_{k}-\sigma^{5}_{k})(\sigma^{3}_{i}-\sigma^{6}_{i})f_{1234}\right\rangle-(P^{2})_{jk}P_{ij}-(P^{2})_{ji}P_{kj}-(P^{2})_{jj}P_{ki}\,.

For the non-repeated third moment, the following identity holds,

\displaystyle\sum_{a}P_{ja}T_{akj}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\left\langle(\sigma^{1}_{j}-\sigma^{2}_{j})(\sigma^{3}_{j}-\sigma^{6}_{j})(\sigma^{3}_{k}-\sigma^{5}_{k})f_{1234}\right\rangle\,.

We will define a “triplet” product of individual
f
site factors as
U_{jk}
(which will show up repeatedly) as

U_{ab}:=(\sigma^{r_{1}}_{a}-\sigma^{r_{2}}_{a})(\sigma^{r_{3}}_{a}-\sigma^{r_{4}}_{a})(\sigma^{r_{3}}_{b}-\sigma^{r_{5}}_{b})\,.

We handle each of the four terms chronologically. For the first term, using the identity above,

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ki}\left(m_{i}\sum_{a}P_{ja}T_{akj}\right)\right]=\frac{\beta^{4}}{2}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ik}m_{i}\left\langle U_{jk}f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=_{\eqref{eq:GOE-ibp}}\frac{\beta^{4}}{2n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{i}(\partial_{ki}\left\langle U_{jk}f_{1234}\right\rangle)+\left\langle U_{jk}f_{1234}\partial_{ki}m_{i}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{4}}{4n}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{6}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\left(\sum_{i}\sigma^{7}_{i}\sigma^{\ell}_{i}\right)\left(\sum_{j,k}U_{jk}\sigma^{\ell}_{k}\right)f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]-\sum_{\ell=1}^{6}\left\langle\sum_{i}\left(\sigma^{7}_{i}\sigma^{\ell}_{i}\sum_{k}U_{ik}\sigma^{\ell}_{k}\right)f_{1234}\right\rangle\right)+\frac{\beta^{4}}{2n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle U_{jk}f_{1234}\partial_{ki}m_{i}\right\rangle\right]\,.

Each term in the first two sums (summed over
\ell
) belongs to (where
B^{n}
is a term bounded by
Cn
and dependent only on the last spins of the replicas)

\mathsf{span}\left\{C(\beta)n^{2}\,\nu\left(f_{1234}\Xi_{cd}\Xi_{ab}\right),C(\beta)n\,\nu\left(B^{n}\Xi_{ab}f_{1234}\right)\right\}\,.

The first term is
O(1)
upon an application of
Proposition
5.21
, and the second sum is
O(1)
by the fact that
|B^{n}|\leqslant Cn
and an application of Cauchy–Schwarz followed by
Theorem
5.1
and
Lemma
5.16
. Similar simplifications symmetrizing the sum over
i
and
j
, and using the facts that
\partial_{ki}m_{i}=\beta(P_{ii}m_{k}-m_{i}P_{ik})
and
\sum_{k}(\sigma^{3}_{k}-\sigma^{5}_{k})(Pm)_{k}=\frac{n^{2}}{2}\left\langle f_{3578}((R_{79}-q^{*})-(R_{89}-q^{*}))\right\rangle
, give that

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{4}}{2n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle U_{jk}f_{1234}\partial_{ki}m_{i}\right\rangle\right]=

\displaystyle\mathsf{span}\left\{C(\beta)n^{2}\nu(f_{1234}\Xi_{ab}\Xi_{cd}),\,C(\beta)n^{2}\nu(f_{1234}f_{abcd}f_{abef}\Xi_{gh}),C(\beta)n\nu(B^{n}\Xi_{ab}f_{1234})\right\}\,.

Each of these terms can be bounded by
Proposition
5.21
, or by invoking
(4,4,4,4)
-Hölder’s or Cauchy–Schwarz with
Theorem
5.1
and
Lemma
5.16
.

For the second term, we use the repeated replica identity and (
4.7
) to simplify as

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}A_{ki}m_{i}\sum_{k}P_{bk}T_{jbj}\right]=\frac{\beta^{4}}{2n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(\partial_{ki}m_{i})U_{jk}f_{1235}\right\rangle+m_{i}(\partial_{ki}\left\langle f_{1235}U_{jk})\right\rangle\right]\,.

This term has, by symmetry, exactly the same structure as the first term and can be bounded as
O(1)
by the exact same argument.

For the third term, we simplify using the second repeated index identity and (
4.7
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ki}\sum_{b}P_{bk}\Gamma_{jbij}\right]=-\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ki}P_{ji}(P^{2})_{kj}\right]-\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[A_{ki}(m_{j}\sum_{b}P_{bk}T_{bij})\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{kj}\partial_{ki}P_{ji}+P_{ji}\partial_{ki}(P^{2})_{kj}\right]-\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ki}\left(m_{j}\sum_{b}P_{bk}T_{bij}\right)\right]\,.

We first simplify the first term using
Lemma
A.2
and the product rule along with some algebra to obtain that

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{kj}\partial_{ki}P_{ji}+P_{ji}\partial_{ki}(P^{2})_{kj}\right]=-\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{kj}P_{ji}P_{ik}\right]-\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{kj}P_{jk}P_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ji})^{2}(P^{2})_{kk}+(P^{2})_{ki}P_{kj}P_{ji}+(P^{2})_{ji}P_{kk}P_{ji}+(P^{2})_{jk}P_{ki}P_{ij}\right]+\mathrm{HO}_{12}+\mathrm{HO}_{13}+\mathrm{HO}_{14}+R^{(3)}_{\text{type}}\,,

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{12}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{kj}m_{k}T_{jii}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{13}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{kj}m_{i}T_{jik}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{14}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{jk}\Gamma_{jiki}\right]\,,

\displaystyle R^{(3)}_{\text{type}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ji}\left(m_{k}\left(\sum_{a}P_{ka}T_{aji}+\sum_{a}P_{ja}T_{kai}\right)+m_{i}\left(\sum_{a}P_{ka}T_{ajk}+\sum_{a}P_{ja}T_{kak}\right)+\sum_{a}P_{ka}\Gamma_{ajik}+\sum_{a}\Gamma_{kaki}\right)\right]\,.

The
R^{(3)}_{\text{type}}
term can be shown to be
O(1)
by arguments exactly analogous to those used to bound
R^{(3)}_{1},R^{(3)}_{2}
and
R^{(3)}_{3}
. We will bound the
\mathrm{HO}_{i}
terms later to be
O(1)
. We show that the remaining “powers-of-
P
” terms are
O(1)
first. Note that

|\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{ji}P_{ik}|=|\sum_{i<j}P_{ji}(P^{3})_{ji}|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\,,

which implies that
\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ji}P_{ik}\right]=O(1)
by (
4.2
). Similarly,

|\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}P_{jk}P_{ii}|=|\sum_{i<j}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{jj}|\leqslant|\sum_{i}P_{ii}\sum_{j}(P^{3})_{jj}|=\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\,,

and so
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]=C(\beta)n\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[(n-\left\langle R_{78}\right\rangle)\left\langle f_{1234}f_{1256}f_{3456}]\right\rangle=O(1)
by (
4.2
) and an invocation of a
(4,4,4,4)
-Hölder’s along with
Lemma
5.16
. The remaining “powers-of-P” terms simplify by symmetrization and trace rewrites as

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ji})^{2}(P^{2})_{kk}+(P^{2})_{ki}P_{kj}P_{ji}+(P^{2})_{ji}P_{kk}P_{ji}+(P^{2})_{jk}P_{ki}P_{ij}\right]=

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\beta^{5}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}](\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]-\sum_{i}(P_{ii})^{2})+\mathsf{Tr}[P](\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]-\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}P_{ii})+\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]-\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\right]\,.

Since
0\leqslant P_{ii}\leqslant 1
and
(P^{k})_{ii}\geqslant 0
, we obtain the facts that

\displaystyle\sum_{i}P^{2}_{ii}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P]\leqslant n\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}=\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]=_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p2-bound}}}\frac{n^{2}}{4}\left\langle f^{2}\right\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]=_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p3-bound}}}\frac{n^{3}}{8}\left\langle f_{1234}f_{1256}f_{3456}\right\rangle\,.

Now, note (
4.2
) implies that
\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]^{2}=\frac{n^{4}}{16}\left\langle f^{2}_{1234}f^{2}_{5678}\right\rangle
. Putting these bounds together gives that

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\left|\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\left(\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]-\sum_{i}(P_{ii})^{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant C(\beta)n^{2}\left\langle f^{2}_{1234}f^{2}_{5678}\right\rangle+C(\beta)n\left\langle f^{2}\right\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\left|\mathsf{Tr}[P]\left(\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]-\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant C(\beta)n^{2}\left\langle f_{1234}f_{1256}f_{3456}\right\rangle+C(\beta)n\nu(f^{2})\,

(4.29)

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\left|\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]-\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p4-bound}}}C(\beta)n^{2}\left\langle f_{1234}f_{5678}f_{1278}f_{3456}\right\rangle+C(\beta)n\left\langle f_{1234}f_{1256}f_{3456}\right\rangle\,.

Taking expectation
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\cdot]
and applying
Proposition
5.21
for the cubic terms, along with Cauchy–Schwarz and
Lemma
5.16
for the other terms yields the
O(1)
bound for the “powers-of-P” terms.

We now bound the final contribution to this term which comes from
\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ki}\left(m_{j}\sum_{b}P_{kb}T_{bij}\right)\right]
by first using (
4.7
) and the identity that
\sum_{b}P_{kb}T_{bik}=\frac{n}{2}\left\langle\Delta^{12}_{k}\Delta^{35}_{i}\Delta^{36}_{j}f_{1234}\right\rangle
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ki}\left(m_{j}\sum_{b}P_{kb}T_{bij}\right)\right]=\frac{\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{k,i}\left(m_{j}\sum_{b}P_{kb}T_{bij}\right)\right]-\frac{\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ki}\left(m_{i}\sum_{b}P_{kb}T_{bii}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ki}\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{j}\Delta^{12}_{k}\Delta^{35}_{i}\Delta^{36}_{j}f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]-\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{i}\left(m_{i}\partial_{ki}(P^{2})_{ki}+(P^{2})_{ki}\partial_{ki}m_{i}\right)\right]\,.

We bound the first term by grouping sums and some algebra multiplying replica indices, which yields

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\partial_{ki}\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{j}\Delta^{12}_{k}\Delta^{35}_{i}\Delta^{36}_{j}f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]=\frac{\beta^{5}}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{7}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\sigma^{7}_{j}\Delta^{12}_{k}\Delta^{35}_{i}\Delta^{36}_{j}f_{1234}(\sigma^{\ell}_{k}\sigma^{\ell}_{i}-\left\langle\sigma_{k}\sigma_{i}\right\rangle)\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=C(\beta)\sum_{\ell=1}^{7}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(R_{37}-R_{67})\left(R_{1\ell}-R_{2\ell}\right)\left(R_{3\ell}-R_{5\ell}\right)f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-C(\beta)\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle(R_{37}-R_{67})(R_{18}-R_{28})(R_{39}-R_{59})f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]\,.

Adding and subtracting
q^{*}
in all the overlap difference terms, one observes that each of these terms can be bounded by a
(4,4,4,4)
-Hölder’s in conjunction with
Theorem
5.1
and
Lemma
5.16
or by
Proposition
5.21
when
\ell\in\{1,2,3,5\}
.
We bound the second term in two parts. For the
(P^{2})_{ki}\partial_{ki}m_{i}
contribution, note that
\partial_{ki}m_{i}=\beta(m_{k}P_{ii}-m_{i}P_{ki})
and so
|\partial_{ki}m_{i}|\leqslant 2\beta
to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}m_{i}(P^{2})_{ki}\partial_{ki}m_{i}\right|\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}|(P^{2})_{ki}|\leqslant_{\text{CS}}n\left(\sum_{i,k}(P^{2})^{2}_{ki}\right)^{1/2}=n\sqrt{\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]}\,.

Taking
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\cdot\right]
and using Jensen’s inequality in conjunction with (
4.2
) yields
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ki}\partial_{ki}m_{i}\right]=O(1)
. Now for the second part, using the product rule and
Lemma
A.2
gives

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m^{2}_{i}\partial_{ki}(P^{2})_{ki}\right]=\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m^{2}_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}P_{kk}+m^{2}_{i}P_{kk}(P^{2})_{ii}+2m^{2}_{i}P_{ik}(P^{2})_{ki}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{i}^{3}\left(\sum_{a}T_{kak}P_{ai}+\sum_{a}P_{ka}T_{aik}\right)+m_{i}^{2}m_{k}\left(\sum_{a}T_{kai}P_{ai}+\sum_{a}P_{ka}T_{aii}\right)\right]}_{:=\mathrm{HO}_{15}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{i}^{2}\sum_{a}\Gamma_{kaki}P_{ai}+m_{i}^{2}\sum_{a}P_{ka}\Gamma_{aiki}\right]}_{:=\mathrm{HO}_{16}}\,.

Once again, we show that
\mathrm{HO}_{15}
and
\mathrm{HO}_{16}
are
O(1)
later, and focus on bounding the “product-of-P” terms first. Note that since
0\leqslant P_{ii}\leqslant 1
and
m^{2}_{i}\leqslant 1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}\left((m^{2}_{i}\left(P^{2})_{ii}P_{kk}+(P^{2})_{kk}P_{ii}\right)\right)\leqslant\sum_{i,k}\left(P^{2})_{ii}P_{kk}+(P^{2})_{kk}P_{ii}\right)=2\mathsf{Tr}[P]\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\,.

Since
\mathsf{Tr}[P]\leqslant n
, this immediately implies that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{2})_{ii}P_{kk}+(P^{2})_{kk}P_{ii}\right]\leqslant O(1)
by (
4.2
). For the remaining term, note that

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}m^{2}_{i}(P^{2})_{ki}P_{ik}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}m^{2}_{i}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{ik}P_{ki}=\sum_{i}m^{2}_{i}(P^{3})_{ii}=\mathsf{Tr}\left[\diag(m^{2})P^{3}\right]\,.

Clearly
0\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[\diag(m^{2})P^{3}]
, and since
\diag(M)\preceq I_{n}
,
\mathsf{Tr}[\diag(m^{2})P^{3}]\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]
. Combining the upper bound with (
4.2
) immediately implies that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m^{2}_{i}(P^{2})_{ki}P_{ik}\right]=O(1)
.

Finally, just as the first and second third-centered cumulant terms were similar by symmetry, the final fourth-cumulant remainder term from
\beta^{2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]]
has exactly the same structure as the one bounded above (by symmetry) and can be shown to be
O(1)
by an exactly analogous argument involving bounding similar terms.

The
\mathrm{HO}_{i}
terms.
The sixteen
\mathrm{HO}
terms can be shown to be
O(1)
using replica identities for the centered third moments and fourth moments followed by tedious symmetrization algebra, ultimately reducing to applications of
Proposition
5.21
or Hölder’s inequality depending on the type of term arising from the simplification. Since the techniques are the same as the ones used for the four terms above, but every term is only slightly different, the details are delegated to
Lemma
A.5
. ∎

Lemma 4.16
(Bounding the
\mathcal{E}_{R}
term as
O(1)
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Define

\mathcal{E}_{R}:=\frac{\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})^{2}P]]-\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[PS(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})P]+\frac{2\beta^{3}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[PA(x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}})P\right]+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{Tr}[x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}P]\,.

Then,

\mathcal{E}_{R}=O_{\beta,t}(1)\,.

Proof 16
.

For the entire proof, we will set (WLOG) the plant
x_{0}=\mathbf{1}_{n}
. Since the function
\|\widehat{Q}^{-1}P-I\|_{F}^{2}
is a continuous and bounded function of the replicas, the proof of
Theorem
5.2
implies that the gauge symmetry and Bayes rule leave it invariant under any fixed plant. Furthermore, observing that
\widehat{Q}^{-1}P-I=\left((S-\beta A)P-I\right)-\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}x_{0}x_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}P
, simplifies
\mathcal{E}_{R}
to the following

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{R}

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert{\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}P}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\langle(S-\beta A)P-I,\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}P\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{Cauchy-Schwarz}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert{\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}P}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+2\left(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert{\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}P}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert{\left((S-\beta A)P-I\right)}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}\right)^{1/2}\,.

Note that
Lemma
4.11
,
Lemma
4.12
,
Lemma
4.13
and
Lemma
4.15
imply that

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert{\left((S-\beta A)P-I\right)}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}=\mathcal{E}_{A}+\mathcal{E}_{D}=O_{\beta,t}(1)\,.

Therefore, it suffices to prove that
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert{\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}P}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}=O_{\beta,t}(1)
to conclude the proof. This will follow by applying replica identities for
P_{ij}
and
(P^{2})_{ij}
using the proof of
Lemma
4.15
with the cubic bounds obtained in
Proposition
5.21
. Specifically,

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert{\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}P}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}\right]\right]=\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left[\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{4}}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]=\frac{\beta^{4}}{4}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\sum_{ij}\left(\sigma^{1}_{i}-\sigma^{2}_{i}\right)\left(\sigma^{3}_{i}-\sigma^{4}_{i}\right)f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(a,b)\in\{(1,3),(1,4),(2,3),(2,4)\}}\frac{\beta^{4}n^{2}}{4}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\left(M_{a}-m^{*}\right)\left(M_{b}-m^{*}\right)f_{1234}\right\rangle\right]=_{\text{\hyperref@@ii[prop:D15]{Proposition~\ref*{prop:D15}}}}O(1)\,,

where we used the fact that

\displaystyle\nu((M_{a}-m^{*})(M_{b}-m^{*})f_{1234})

\displaystyle=\nu((M^{-}_{a}-m^{*})(M^{-}_{b}-m^{*})f_{1234})+\frac{1}{n}\nu((M^{-}_{a}-m^{*})\varepsilon^{b}f_{1234})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\nu((M^{-}_{b}-m^{*})\varepsilon^{a}f_{1234})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\nu(\varepsilon^{a}\varepsilon^{b}f_{1234})\,,

and the first term can be bounded directly by
Proposition
5.21
, the second and third term using Cauchy-Schwarz along with the facts that
|\varepsilon^{a}|\leqslant 1
,
|\varepsilon^{b}|\leqslant 1
,
Lemma
5.16
and overlap/magnetization concentration, and the last term is calculated by trivially using the boundedness of
|\varepsilon^{a}\varepsilon^{b}|\leqslant 1
.

Corollary 4.17
(Final bound on the Frobenius norm)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

The following bounds hold for the three terms constituting the Frobenius norm,

\mathcal{E}_{A}+\mathcal{E}_{D}+\mathcal{E}_{R}=O(1)\,.

Proof 17
.

The proof follows from
Lemma
4.11
,
Lemma
4.12
,
Lemma
4.13
,
Lemma
4.15
and
Lemma
4.16
.

5.
Exact moments estimates via planted cavity interpolation on the Nishimori line

In this section, we first state the main concentration estimates of the magnetization and overlap (
Theorem
5.1
,
Theorem
5.2
) that are known for the planted SK model under SL tilt up to
\beta<1
. These mainly reduce to showing that a mapping from the planted SK model under SL tilt to a Guerra interpolated model in
[
7
, §4]
preserves certain key properties. We then establish moment bounds (under the disorder) on every entry of the diagonal matrix
D
that appears in the Hessian of the TAP free energy (
Lemma
5.3
). With access to these lemmata and a few others regarding replica algebra (
§
A.    Replica identities and higher-order remainder terms
), we introduce the cavity interpolation for the planted SK model with SL tilt (
§
5.4
). Under this interpolation, we systematically develop various exact moment estimates and strengthened concentration bounds, ultimately culminating in the four
critical
results of this section. These are:

•

Proposition
5.23
and
Lemma
5.30
, which provide
exact
estimates for
O(1/n)
terms that appear in
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert{\widehat{Q}^{-1}P-I_{n}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}
to account for the “heavy” cancellations, and

•

Proposition
5.21
and
Lemma
5.27
, which are concentration estimates that beat “naive” Hölder bounds by using extra cancellations. These are critically used to bound various remainder terms that come from an interaction with the diagonal matrix
D
or mixed cubic moments of “bulk” deviation terms.

5.1.
Overlap concentration in the planted SK Gibbs measure

We state a critical theorem in this section, which implies that the moments of the overlap variance for the Gibbs measure of the planted SK model can be bounded by a constant that depends only on
\beta
and the moment parameter
k
.

Theorem 5.1
(Overlap concentration for the planted SK model,
[
7
, Mild extension of Theorem 7]
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

For
G_{A,\beta,y_{t}}
,
0<\beta<1
, and every positive integer
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\langle(R_{12}-q^{*})^{2k}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{C(k)}{n^{k}}+C(k)e^{-c(k,t)N}\,,

for positive constants
C(k)
and
c(k,t)
, where

q^{*}=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{h\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}\left[\tanh^{2}\left(\beta^{2}m^{*}+t+\sqrt{\beta^{2}q^{*}+t}h\right)\right]\,,

and

m^{*}=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{h\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}\left[\tanh\left(\beta^{2}m^{*}+t+\sqrt{\beta^{2}q^{*}+t}h\right)\right]\,.

Proof 18
.

This is a mild generalization of
[
7
, Theorem 7]
and the remark on
[
7
, Pg. 13]
after the statement of
[
7
, Theorem 8]
. El Alaoui, Krzakala and Jordan consider a general setup that handles the planted SK model without SL tilt and observe that their Guerra interpolation over
s\in[0,1]
preserves a
Nishimori property
such that the time-
s
interpolated Gibbs measure corresponds to the posterior distribution of the plant vector given
additional
scalar side channel information

y_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}\left((1-s)r,(1-s)r\right)\,,

where
r=\lambda q^{*}(\lambda)
, with
q^{*}(\lambda)
being the unique solution achieving
\sup_{q\geqslant 0}F(\lambda,q)

[
86
, (3) & §2.3]
.

We quickly show that for every
0<\beta<1
and
t\in[0,T(\beta)]
there exists a unique pair
(\lambda,s)
that satisfies the the following requirements:

(1)

s\in[0,1]
,

(2)

\lambda s=\beta^{2}
,

(3)

q^{*}(\lambda)=q^{*}
,

(4)

0<\beta^{2}\leqslant\lambda<\infty
(and so
\lambda\in\mathcal{D}
) ,

(5)

r=\lambda q^{*}(\lambda)
,

(6)

t=(1-s)r
, and

(7)

H_{s}\overset{d}{=}H_{\text{planted}}
.

Parameter map.
The map sets
(\beta,t)\to\left(\lambda=\beta^{2}+\frac{t}{q^{*}},s=\frac{\beta^{2}q^{*}}{\beta^{2}q^{*}+t}\right)
when
t>0
. For the case of no tilt at
t=0
, we map
(\beta,t)\to(\lambda=\beta^{2},s=1)
. In both cases,
r:=\beta^{2}q^{*}+t
. Since
0\leqslant t\leqslant T(\beta)<\infty
, it is immediate that
s\in[0,1)
. Furthermore,
\lambda s=\beta^{2}
for every
t\in[0,T(\beta)]
.

Fixed-point equivalence.
The case of
t=0
and
t>0
are handled separately.

(1)

When
t=0
, note that
\lambda=\beta^{2}<1
and so the
q^{*}(\lambda)
that extermizes
F_{\lambda,q}
is at
q^{*}(\lambda)=0

[
86
, §2.3]
. For
t=0
, it is also easy to see that
q^{*}=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{h}\left[\tanh^{2}\left(\beta^{2}m^{*}+\sqrt{\beta^{2}q^{*}}h\right)\right]
. Since
m^{*}=q^{*}
by
Lemma
5.32
, it is immediate that
q^{*}=0
. This yields
q^{*}(\lambda)=q^{*}
.

(2)

At
t>0
, we use
Lemma
5.32
and the fact that
q^{*}=f(\beta^{2}q^{*}+t)<\beta^{2}q^{*}+t=r=\lambda q^{*}
to conclude that
\lambda>1
and so
[
86
, (9) & §2.3]
implies that
q^{*}(\lambda)=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[X^{2}_{0}]-\left(1-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\tanh^{2}(\langle\lambda q^{*}(\lambda)+\sqrt{\lambda q^{*}(\lambda)}h)\right]\right)
. Note that
x_{0}\sim\mathsf{Unif}\left(\{-1,1\}^{n}\right)
corresponds to the case that
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[X_{0}^{2}]=1
which immediately implies that
q^{*}(\lambda)=q^{*}
.

We now show that
f(r)=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{h}[\tanh^{2}(r+\sqrt{r}h)]<r
. Note that, for
g(x)=x-\tanh(x)
(which is odd) we have

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(x)=1-\sech^{2}(x)=\tanh^{2}(x)\geqslant 0\,,

which implies that
g(x)>0
when
x>0
, and
g(x)<0
for
x<0
. Then, for a Gaussian
Y\sim\mathcal{N}(r,r)
with density
\rho_{r}(x)dx
, we obtain that

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{Y}\left[g(Y)\right]=\int_{0}^{\infty}g(x)\left(\rho_{r}(x)-\rho_{r}(-x)\right)\,dx\,,

and since

\frac{\rho_{r}(x)}{\rho_{r}(-x)}=e^{2x}>1\,,

and
g(x)>0
for
x>0
, we immediately obtain
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{Y}[g(Y)]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[Y-\tanh(Y)]=r-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{Y}[g_{Y}]>0
. By the proof of
Lemma
5.32
, note that
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{Y}[g(Y)]=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{Y}[g^{2}(Y)]=f(r)
, which immediately gives
f(r)<r
for
r>0
.

Invariance of constraints on
r
and
t
.
Once again, we verify the constraints separately for
t=0
and
t>0
.

(1)

At
t=0
, note that
\lambda=\beta^{2}
and the mapping sets
r=\beta^{2}q^{*}+0=0
(since
q^{*}=0
at
t=0
). Since
\lambda=\beta^{2}
and
q^{*}(\lambda)=q^{*}
, we also obtain that
r=\lambda q^{*}(\lambda)=\beta^{2}\cdot 0=0
, which preserves the desired invariance. Similarly, with
r=0
, we obtain that
t=(1-s)r=0
, which is also consistent.

(2)

At
t>0
, we have that the mapping sets
\lambda=\beta^{2}+t/q^{*}
and
r=\beta^{2}q^{*}+t=(\beta^{2}+t/q^{*})q^{*}=\lambda q^{*}=\lambda q^{*}(\lambda)
, where the last equality follows because
q^{*}=q^{*}(\lambda)
. Similarly,
(1-s)r=(1-s)\lambda q^{*}(\lambda)=\left(1-\frac{\beta^{2}q^{*}}{\beta^{2}q^{*}+t}\right)(\beta^{2}+t/q^{*})q^{*}=t
, as required.

Distributional equivalence of the Hamiltonians.
Given a fixed plant
x_{0}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}
, note that by the distributional equivalences used for
(\mathsf{diag}(x_{0})J\mathsf{diag}(x_{0}),\mathsf{diag}(x_{0})y_{t},\mathsf{diag}(x_{0})\,\sigma)
in the proof of
Theorem
5.2
, we have that

H_{\text{planted}}(\tau)\overset{d}{=}\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}\tau_{i}\tau_{j}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2n}\left(\sum_{i}u_{i}\right)^{2}+t\sum_{i}u_{i}+\sqrt{t}\sum_{i}z_{i}u_{i}\,,

where
\tau=\diag(x_{0})\sigma
,
z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n})
, and we remove the diagonal entries because of
Lemma
A.1
. Furthermore, by
[
7
, (22)]
, we have

H_{s}(\tau)\overset{d}{=}\sqrt{\frac{s\lambda}{n}}\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}\tau_{i}\tau_{j}+\frac{s\lambda}{n}\sum_{i<j}u_{i}u_{j}+\sqrt{(1-s)r}\sum_{i}z_{i}u_{i}+(1-s)r\sum_{i}u_{i}\,,

after the dropping the
u_{i}
-independent constant terms (using the fact that
u_{i}^{2}=1
). After symmetrizing the sum over
i
and
j
, ignoring constants, and using
s\lambda=\beta^{2}
and
(1-s)r=t
, we immediately obtain that
H_{s}(\tau)\overset{d}{=}H_{\text{planted}}(\tau)
.

Given the proof of the requirements above, it immediately follows that
[
7
, Theorem 7]
and the remark on
[
7
, Pg. 13]
apply verbatim to
H_{\text{planted}}
.

5.2.
Magnetization concentration in the planted SK Gibbs measure

This is obtained as a consequence of the overlap concentration achieved by the Guerra interpolation in
[
7
, §4]
. The key insight is that the symmetry implied by the map
\sigma\to x_{0}\circ\sigma
being a bijection on
\{-1,1\}^{n}
combines with the Nishimori identity (
m^{*}=q^{*}
) and the Bayes rule to give magnetization concentration. This is, in fact, a remark made in
[
7
, §3]
and we prove it for completeness.

Theorem 5.2
(Magnetization concentration for the planted SK model)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Fix the plant
x_{0}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}
and let
\beta\in(0,1/2)
. Then, setting
M:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(x_{0})_{i}\sigma_{i}
, under the Nishimori condition that
m^{*}=q^{*}
where
(m^{*},q^{*})
solve the fixed-point equations in
Theorem
5.1
, for every positive integer
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, there is a constant
C(\beta,k)
so that

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\langle(M-m^{*})^{2k}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{C(k)}{n^{k}}+C(\beta,k)e^{-c(k,t)N}\,.

Proof 19
.

Note that
M=R_{1,x_{0}}
, which is the overlap between a single replica and the planted vector
x_{0}
. Denote the contribution to the quadratic term for every
i<j
as

J_{ij}:=\beta A_{ij}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}(x_{0})_{i}(x_{0})_{j}\,.

Since, for
i=j
,
\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}=\sigma^{2}_{i}=1
, by
Lemma
A.1
we have that
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\langle f\right\rangle
is unchanged under the diagonal entries of
A
for any measurable function
f
. Given the fixed plant
x_{0}
, the following distributional equivalences hold

\displaystyle J_{ij}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}(x_{0})_{i}(x_{0})_{j},\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\right)\,,

\displaystyle(y_{t})_{i}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}\left(t(x_{0})_{i},t\right)\,.

Equivalence for a uniform plant via Bayes’ rule.
At this point, choose
x\sim\mathsf{Unif}\left(\{-1,1\}^{n}\right)
, and conditioned on the disorder
\{J_{ij}\}_{i<j}
and the field
y_{t}
, observe the following via Bayes’ rule

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left\{x=\sigma\mid\{J_{ij}\}_{i<j},y_{t}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{-n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left\{\{J_{ij}\}_{i<j},y_{t}\mid x=\sigma\right\}}{2^{-n}\sum_{x}\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left\{\{J_{ij}\}_{i<j},y_{t}\mid x=\sigma\right\}}=\frac{2^{-n}e^{\langle\sigma,J\sigma\rangle+\langle y_{t},\sigma\rangle}}{Z}\,.

Consequently, given iid replicas
(\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2})
drawn from
\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left\{x\mid\{J_{ij}\}_{i<j},y_{t}\right\}
,

\left(J,y_{t},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2}\right)\overset{d}{=}\left(J,y_{t},\sigma^{1},x\right)\,,

which follows immediately by
[
26
, Lemma 1]
. Since
(M-m^{*})^{2k}
is a continuous and bounded function, it immediately implies that

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{x}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A}\left\langle(R_{12}-q^{*})^{2k}\right\rangle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{x}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A}\left\langle(M-m^{*})^{2k}\right\rangle\leqslant C(\beta,k)\,,

since
m^{*}=q^{*}
(
Lemma
5.32
).

Equivalence for a fixed plant via gauge symmetry.
Using the closure of the diagonal algebra and the invariance of Gaussians to signs, we can extend the result above to
every
fixed plant
x_{0}
. Set

\displaystyle J^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{diag}\left(x_{0}\right)J\mathsf{diag}\left(x_{0}\right)\,,

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{diag}\left(x_{0}\right)y_{t}\,,

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{diag}\left(x_{0}\right)\sigma\,.

The matrix
\mathsf{diag}(x_{0})
is clearly its own inverse, and consequently

\langle\sigma,J\sigma\rangle+\langle y_{t},\sigma\rangle=\langle\tau,J^{\prime}\tau\rangle+\langle h^{\prime},\tau\rangle\,.

Conditioning on
x=x_{0}
gives the following law for
J^{\prime}
and
y^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle J^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{diag}(x_{0})\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}xx^{\mathsf{T}}\diag(x_{0})+\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\diag(x_{0})A\diag(x_{0})

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\mathsf{T}}+\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}A^{\prime}\,,

where
A^{\prime}\overset{d}{=}A\sim\mathsf{GOE}(n)
, and

y^{\prime}_{t}=\diag(x_{0})y_{t}\overset{d}{=}t\mathbf{1}_{n}+B^{\prime}_{t}\,,

where
B^{\prime}_{t}\overset{d}{=}B_{t}
. This immediately implies that the following conditional law is the same,

(J^{\prime},y^{\prime}_{t})\mid x=x_{0}\overset{d}{=}(J,y_{t})\mid x=\mathbf{1}_{n}\,.

Since
x\sim\mathsf{Unif}\left(\{-1,1\}^{n}\right)
, one can substitute the distributional equivalence above into the Nishimori expectation equivalence over
x
to conclude that

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A}\left\langle(M-m^{*})^{2k}\right\rangle=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A}\left\langle(R_{1,2}-m^{*})^{2k}\right\rangle\leqslant C(\beta,k)\,,

for
every

x_{0}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}
.

5.3.
Uniform bounds on bulk statistics of
D(m)

It will be critical to have cavity interpolations for bulk observables that are weighted by terms of
D(m)
, which will require uniform control over the moments of the entries of the diagonal matrix. This will be useful in various applications of Hölder’s inequality in statements that control odd moments of the form
\nu_{s}(\widehat{f}^{-}G)
, where
G
is a quadratic term involving centered overlaps or magnetizations.

Lemma 5.3
(Uniform-in-
s
moments of
D_{ii}
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Fix
t\in[0,T]
and
0<\beta<1/2
. For each
s\in[0,1]
let
\langle\cdot\rangle_{s}

denote the Gibbs average along the cavity interpolation Hamiltonian defined in
§
5.4
. Set

m_{i}(s):=\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle_{s},\qquad D_{ii}(s):=\frac{1}{1-m_{i}(s)^{2}}=\cosh^{2}(\operatorname{arctanh}(m_{i}(s)))\,.

Then, for every
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, there exists a constant
C_{p,\beta,t}
such that

(5.1)

\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\ \sup_{i\in[n]}\ \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[D_{ii}(s)^{p}\big]\ \leqslant\ C_{p,\beta,t}.

Consequently,

\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\ \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathsf{Tr}\big(D(m(s))^{p}\big)\Big]=\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[D_{ii}(s)^{p}\big]\leqslant C_{p,\beta,t}\,n.

Proof.

Let the time of the interpolation
s\in[0,1]
, and fix a spin
i\in[n]
. Split the Hamiltonian to isolate the cavity field for
i
as

(5.2)

\beta H_{s}(\sigma)=\beta H^{(i)}_{s}(\sigma_{-i})+\sigma_{i}\Big(y_{t,i}+H^{\mathrm{row}}_{i,s}(\sigma_{-i})+H^{\mathrm{bd}}_{i}(\sigma_{-i})\Big),

where, conditional on all disorder variables not in the
i
-th row/column,

H^{\mathrm{row}}_{i,s}(\sigma_{-i})=2\sqrt{s}\,\beta\sum_{k\neq i}(A_{ik})\sigma_{k}\quad\text{with}\quad(A_{ik})_{k\neq i}\overset{iid}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1/n),

and the remaining
\sigma_{i}
–linear contribution is uniformly bounded:

(5.3)

\sup_{\sigma_{-i}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n-1}}\big|H^{\mathrm{bd}}_{i}(\sigma_{-i})\big|\leqslant C_{\beta,t}<\infty\,.

Note that with a fixed plant
x_{0}\equiv\mathbf{1}
, one can take
H^{\mathrm{bd}}_{i}(\sigma_{-i})=(\beta^{2}/n)\sum_{k\neq i}\sigma_{k}
. This immediately implies that
C_{\beta,t}=\beta^{2}
.

Single-spin marginal and the
\cosh^{2}
representation.
Define the constrained (bulk) partition functions

Z_{i,s}(\pm):=\sum_{\sigma_{-i}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n-1}}\exp\Big(\beta H^{(i)}_{s}(\sigma_{-i})\pm\big(H^{\mathrm{row}}_{i,s}(\sigma_{-i})+H^{\mathrm{bd}}_{i}(\sigma_{-i})\big)\Big).

Then, the computation of the single-spin marginal using the identity
\tan(x)=(e^{2x}-1)/(e^{2x}+1)
gives

m_{i}(s)=\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle_{s}=\tanh\big(y_{t,i}+U_{i,s}\big)\,

where

U_{i,s}:=\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{Z_{i,s}(+)}{Z_{i,s}(-)}\,.

This immediately gives

D_{ii}(s)=\sech^{-2}\big(y_{t,i}+U_{i,s}\big)=\cosh^{2}\big(y_{t,i}+U_{i,s}\big)\,,

and the following upper bound for any
p
-th power,

(5.4)

D_{ii}(s)^{p}=\cosh^{2p}\big(y_{t,i}+U_{i,s}\big)\leqslant\exp\big(2p\,|y_{t,i}+U_{i,s}|\big)\leqslant\exp\big(2p|y_{t,i}|\big)\exp\big(2p|U_{i,s}|\big).

Note that the Gaussians in
y_{t,i}
and
U_{i,s}
are independent of each other, and so it suffices to show that
y_{t,i}
and
U_{i,s}
are sub-Gaussian random variables to ensure that the MGFs in the RHS of the inequality above are bounded.

Bounding
U_{i,s}
via conditional Gaussian concentration.
Condition on all the disorder
except
the Gaussian row
(A_{ik})_{k\neq i}
, and exclude
y_{t,i}
.
Under this conditioning, the vector

g:=(g_{k})_{k\neq i},\qquad g_{k}:=\sqrt{n}\,A_{ik},

is standard Gaussian in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
, and
U_{i,s}
is a smooth function of
g
.

Let
\langle\cdot\rangle_{i,s}^{\pm}
denote the Gibbs average on
\sigma_{-i}
with weight proportional to

\exp(\beta H^{(i)}_{s}(\sigma_{-i})\pm(H^{\mathrm{row}}_{i,s}+H^{\mathrm{bd}}_{i}))
.
Differentiating
\log Z_{i,s}(\pm)
w.r.t.
A_{ik}
gives

\partial_{A_{ik}}\log Z_{i,s}(+)=2\sqrt{s}\,\beta\,\langle\sigma_{k}\rangle_{i,s}^{+},\qquad\partial_{A_{ik}}\log Z_{i,s}(-)=-2\sqrt{s}\,\beta\,\langle\sigma_{k}\rangle_{i,s}^{-}.

Therefore

\partial_{A_{ik}}U_{i,s}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\partial_{A_{ik}}\log Z_{i,s}(+)-\partial_{A_{ik}}\log Z_{i,s}(-)\Big)=2\sqrt{s}\,\beta\Big(\langle\sigma_{k}\rangle_{i,s}^{+}+\langle\sigma_{k}\rangle_{i,s}^{-}\Big),

and since
|\langle\sigma_{k}\rangle_{i,s}^{\pm}|\leqslant 1
we have the uniform bound

\big|\partial_{A_{ik}}U_{i,s}\big|\leqslant 2\sqrt{s}\,\beta\leqslant 2\beta.

Passing to the standard Gaussian coordinates
g_{k}=\sqrt{n}A_{ik}
yields the following per-coordinate gradient bound,

\big|\partial_{g_{k}}U_{i,s}\big|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big|\partial_{A_{ik}}U_{i,s}\big|\leqslant\frac{2\beta}{\sqrt{n}}.

This gives the following bound for the Lipschitz-constant of the gradient,

\|\nabla_{g}U_{i,s}\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{k\neq i}\big(\partial_{g_{k}}U_{i,s}\big)^{2}\leqslant\sum_{k\neq i}\frac{4\beta^{2}}{n}\leqslant 4\beta^{2}.

By concentration for Lipschitz functions of standard Gaussians
[
34
, Theorem 5.5]
,
this implies that conditioning on all
\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}
,

(5.5)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[\exp\big(\lambda\left(U_{i,s}-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[U_{i,s}\mid\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}]\right)\big)\,\Big|\,\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}\Big]\leqslant\exp\Big(2\lambda^{2}\beta^{2}\Big)\,,

for every
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{\geqslant 0}
.
In particular, for any
\alpha>0
, using
e^{\alpha|x|}\leqslant e^{\alpha x}+e^{-\alpha x}
and (
5.5
),

(5.6)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[\exp\big(\alpha\left|U_{i,s}-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[U_{i,s}\mid\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}]\right|\big)\,\Big|\,\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}\Big]\leqslant 2\exp\Big(2\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}\Big).

Bounding the conditional mean via the bounded
\sigma_{i}
–linear remainder.
Let
U^{(0)}_{i,s}
denote the same constrained free energy ratio as
U_{i,s}
but with
H^{\mathrm{bd}}_{i}\equiv 0
, which comes by removing the “planted” cavity term from (
5.2
). Then
Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(\pm)
satisfy

Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(+;A_{i\cdot})=Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(-;-A_{i\cdot}),

and therefore
U^{(0)}_{i,s}(-A_{i\cdot})=-U^{(0)}_{i,s}(A_{i\cdot})
.
Since
(A_{ij})_{i=k\text{ or }j=k}
are centered Gaussians, it follows that, conditional on
\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}
,

(5.7)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[U^{(0)}_{i,s}\mid\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}\big]=0\,,

since it is an odd function.

Now, we compare
U_{i,s}
and
U^{(0)}_{i,s}
.
Using (
5.3
), for every configuration
\sigma_{-i}
, one obtains that

e^{-C_{\beta,t}}\leqslant e^{\pm H^{\mathrm{bd}}_{i}(\sigma_{-i})}\leqslant e^{C_{\beta,t}}
.
Therefore,

e^{-C_{\beta,t}}Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(+)\leqslant Z_{i,s}(+)\leqslant e^{C_{\beta,t}}Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(+),\qquad e^{-C_{\beta,t}}Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(-)\leqslant Z_{i,s}(-)\leqslant e^{C_{\beta,t}}Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(-).

Taking ratios and logs gives the deterministic bound

(5.8)

\big|U_{i,s}-U^{(0)}_{i,s}\big|=\frac{1}{2}\left|\log\frac{Z_{i,s}(+)/Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(+)}{Z_{i,s}(-)/Z^{(0)}_{i,s}(-)}\right|\leqslant C_{\beta,t}.

Combining (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) via a triangle inequality gives the following bound on the conditional mean,

(5.9)

\big|\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[U_{i,s}\mid\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}]\big|\leqslant\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[|U_{i,s}-U^{(0)}_{i,s}|\mid\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}\big]\leqslant C_{\beta,t}.

Exponential moment bound for
|U_{i,s}|
.
By the triangle inequality,

|U_{i,s}|\leqslant|U_{i,s}-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[U_{i,s}\mid\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}]|+|\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[U_{i,s}\mid\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}]|.

Hence, for any
\alpha>0
, using (
5.9
) and then (
5.6
),

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[e^{\alpha|U_{i,s}|}\,\Big|\,\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}\Big]\leqslant e^{\alpha C_{\beta,t}}\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[e^{\alpha|U_{i,s}-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[U_{i,s}\mid\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}]|}\,\Big|\,\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}\Big]\leqslant 2\,\exp\Big(\alpha C_{\beta,t}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}{2}\Big).

Taking expectation over
\{A_{kj}\}_{k\neq i,j\neq i}
gives the unconditional bound

(5.10)

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[e^{\alpha|U_{i,s}|}\big]\leqslant 2\,\exp\Big(\alpha C_{\beta,t}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}{2}\Big),\qquad\forall\alpha>0\,.

Using (
5.4
), the independence of
y_{t,i}
from
\{A_{ij}\}
,
and (
5.10
) with
\alpha=2p
,

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[D_{ii}(s)^{p}\big]\leqslant\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[e^{2p|y_{t,i}|}\big]\;\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[e^{2p|U_{i,s}|}\big]\leqslant\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[e^{2p|y_{t,i}|}\big]\left(2\exp\Big(2p\,C_{\beta,t}+2p^{2}\beta^{2}\Big)\right)=:C_{p,\beta,t}\,.\qed

5.4.
Exact overlap moment estimates via a modified cavity interpolation

Since
\widehat{R}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
is centered replica-by-replica (sitewise, for each disorder), all mixed covariances that force Talagrand to introduce the auxiliary variables
T_{\ell},T
in
[
109
, §1.8]
vanish
exactly
as in
Lemma
5.4
. This substantially simplifies the linear system for joint fluctuations: the only nontrivial constant that survives in the cavity recursions is the replicon coefficient
\rho=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\sech^{4}(Y^{*})]
. Thus, to generalize
[
109
, §1.8]
to the setting of the planted SK model under the SL tilt, we work directly with the family
\left\{\sqrt{N}\widehat{R}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\right\}_{\ell<\ell^{\prime}}
.

Rectangular sums for recentered and rescaled overlaps

Let

\beta H_{N}(\sigma):=\frac{\beta}{2}\langle\sigma,A\sigma\rangle+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2N}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sigma_{i}\Big)^{2}+\langle y_{t},\sigma\rangle,\qquad\sigma\in\{-1,+1\}^{N},

where
A\sim\mathsf{GOE}(n)
,
y_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(t,t)

and denote the (expected) Gibbs average under
G_{A,y_{t},\beta}
as
\nu(\cdot):=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\langle\cdot\rangle
. Set
0<\beta<1/2
and assume:

(H1)

Overlap concentration:
For every
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\langle\left(R_{1,2}-q^{*}\right)^{2k}\right\rangle\leqslant C(\beta,k)/n^{k}
.

(H2)

Magnetization concentration:
An analogous bound holds for
the global magnetization
M:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\sigma_{i}
around
m^{*}
.

(H3)

Nishimori condition:

m^{*}=q^{*}
and
(m^{*},q^{*})
solve the fixed-point equations in
Theorem
5.1
.

For each site,

m_{i}:=\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle,\qquad s_{i}^{2}:=1-m_{i}^{2},\qquad\widehat{\sigma}_{i}:=\frac{\sigma_{i}-m_{i}}{s_{i}}.

For replicas
\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\dots
sampled i.i.d. from
G_{A,y_{t},\beta}
, set

\widehat{R}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{\ell}\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{\ell^{\prime}}.

Define the two “rectangular sums” as in
[
109
, §1.8]
:

(5.11)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle:=R_{1,3}-R_{1,4}-R_{2,3}+R_{2,4}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}a_{i},\qquad a_{i}:=(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})(\sigma_{i}^{3}-\sigma_{i}^{4}),

(5.12)

\displaystyle\widehat{f}

\displaystyle:=\widehat{R}_{1,3}-\widehat{R}_{1,4}-\widehat{R}_{2,3}+\widehat{R}_{2,4}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{a}_{i},\qquad\widehat{a}_{i}:=(\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{1}-\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{2})(\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{3}-\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{4})\,,

and similarly for the “cavity” overlaps,

f^{-}:=R^{-}_{1,3}-R^{-}_{1,4}-R^{-}_{2,3}+R^{-}_{2,4},\qquad\widehat{f}^{-}:=\widehat{R}^{-}_{1,3}-\widehat{R}^{-}_{1,4}-\widehat{R}^{-}_{2,3}+\widehat{R}^{-}_{2,4},

where
\widehat{R}^{-}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\leqslant n-1}\widehat{\rho}_{i}^{\ell}\widehat{\rho}_{i}^{\ell^{\prime}}
and
R^{-}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\leqslant n-1}\rho_{i}^{\ell}\rho_{i}^{\ell^{\prime}}
with

\widehat{\rho}_{i}^{\ell}=(\rho_{i}^{\ell}-\langle\rho_{i}\rangle)/\sqrt{1-\langle\rho_{i}\rangle^{2}}
.

We also use the site-decomposition

f=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i},\qquad a_{i}:=(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})(\sigma_{i}^{3}-\sigma_{i}^{4}),\qquad\widehat{f}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{a}_{i},\qquad\widehat{a}_{i}:=(\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{1}-\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{2})(\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{3}-\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{4}).

O-notation

As in Talagrand
[
109
, §1.6 & §1.8]
we write
O(k)
for a quantity bounded by
Kn^{-k/2}
,
where
K
may depend on
(\beta,t)
but not on
n
.

Lemma 5.4
(Exact orthogonality for
\widehat{R}
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Fix disorder
(A,y_{t})
. For i.i.d. replicas
\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\sigma^{3},\sigma^{4}
,

\langle\widehat{R}_{1,2}\widehat{R}_{1,3}\rangle=0,\qquad\langle\widehat{R}_{1,2}\widehat{R}_{3,4}\rangle=0.

Consequently,

(5.13)

\langle\widehat{f}^{2}\rangle=4\langle\widehat{R}_{1,2}^{2}\rangle.

Proof.

Condition on
\sigma^{1}
and use that replicas are independent given disorder and

\langle\widehat{\sigma}_{i}\rangle=0
by definition; thus

\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{R}_{1,2}\mid\sigma^{1}]=0
, giving
\langle\widehat{R}_{1,2}\widehat{R}_{1,3}\rangle=0
.
The disjoint-pair identity follows from
\langle\widehat{R}_{1,2}\rangle=0
.
Expanding
\widehat{f}^{2}
and using these vanishing cross-covariances leaves
four identical square terms, giving (
5.13
).
∎

Modified cavity interpolation for the planted SK model

Write
\sigma=(\rho,\varepsilon)
with
\rho\in\{-1,1\}^{N-1}
and
\varepsilon=\sigma_{N}
.
Decompose
A
into blocks

A=\begin{pmatrix}A^{--}&a\\
a^{\mathsf{T}}&A_{nn}\end{pmatrix},\qquad a_{i}:=A_{in}.

Then (up to additive terms of scale
O(1/n)
independent of
\varepsilon
)

(5.14)

\beta H_{n}(\rho,\varepsilon)=\beta H_{n-1}^{(n)}(\rho)+\varepsilon\left[\beta\sum_{i\leqslant n-1}a_{i}\rho_{i}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\leqslant n-1}\rho_{i}+y_{t,n}\right],

where
\beta H_{N-1}^{(N)}(\rho)
contains the
(N-1)
-spin SK Hamiltonian,
the CW term on
\rho
, and the external field on
\rho
.

The modified cavity interpolation interpolates the SK field

\sum a_{i}\rho_{i}
with an independent Gaussian field matched at
q^{*}
, and linearly couples the magnetization term
\sum_{i}\rho^{i}
with
m^{*}
.

Let
g_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
i.i.d. and
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
independent. For
s\in[0,1]
, denote the interpolated Hamiltonian as

(5.15)

\beta H_{n,s}(\rho,\varepsilon):=\beta H_{n-1}^{(n)}(\rho)+\varepsilon\left[\sqrt{s}\,\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leqslant n-1}g_{i}\rho_{i}+\sqrt{1-s}\,\beta\sqrt{q^{*}}\,Z+\beta^{2}\left(\frac{s}{n}\sum_{i\leqslant n-1}\rho_{i}+(1-s)m^{*}\right)+y_{t,n}\right].

Let
\langle\cdot\rangle_{s}
denote the Gibbs average and
\nu_{s}(\cdot)=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\langle\cdot\rangle_{s}
.

Lemma 5.5
(Exact factorization at
s=0

[
109
, Lemma 1.6.2 for modified cavity interpolation]
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
F^{-}
be any bounded measurable function of the first
n-1
spins in
k
replicas

\rho^{1},\dots,\rho^{k}
, and let
\Phi
be any bounded measurable function of the last spins

(\varepsilon^{1},\dots,\varepsilon^{k})
. Then under the modified cavity interpolation ,

\nu_{0}\left(F^{-}\,\Phi(\varepsilon^{1},\dots,\varepsilon^{k})\right)=\nu_{0}(F^{-})\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big\langle\Phi(\widetilde{\varepsilon}^{1},\dots,\widetilde{\varepsilon}^{k})\big\rangle_{Y^{*}}\Big],

where conditionally on
Y^{*}
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}^{1},\dots,\widetilde{\varepsilon}^{k}
are i.i.d.
\pm 1

with mean
\tanh(Y^{*})
.
In particular, for any index set
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,k\}
,

\nu_{0}\left(F^{-}\prod_{\ell\in I}\varepsilon^{\ell}\right)=\nu_{0}(F^{-})\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\big[\tanh^{|I|}(Y^{*})\big].

Proof.

At
s=0
the interpolated Hamiltonian becomes

\beta H_{n,0}(\rho,\varepsilon)=\beta H_{n-1}(\rho)+\varepsilon\,Y^{*},\qquad Y^{*}=y_{n}+\beta^{2}m^{*}+\beta\sqrt{q^{*}}\,Z,

and
Y^{*}
is independent of
\rho
. Applying the proof of
[
109
, Lemma 1.6.2]
directly yields the result.
∎

There is a similar analogue for
[
109
, Lemma 1.6.3]
with the exception that the linear part of the interpolation for the magnetization term pulls out a “drift” term. Denote
M^{-}_{\ell}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<n}\rho^{\ell}_{i}
.

Lemma 5.6
(Derivative identity with CW drift
[
109
, Lemma 1.6.3 for modified cavity interpolation]
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
F
be bounded and depend on
k
replicas
(\rho^{1},\varepsilon^{1}),\dots,(\rho^{k},\varepsilon^{k})
. Then, for
s\in(0,1)
,

(5.16)

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}_{s}(F)=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\beta^{2}\sum_{1\leqslant\ell<\ell^{\prime}\leqslant k}\nu_{s}\left(F\,\varepsilon^{\ell}\varepsilon^{\ell^{\prime}}(R^{-}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}-q^{*})\right)-\beta^{2}k\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\nu_{s}\left(F\,\varepsilon^{\ell}\varepsilon^{k+1}(R^{-}_{\ell,k+1}-q^{*})\right)+\frac{\beta^{2}k(k+1)}{2}\nu_{s}\left(F\,\varepsilon^{k+1}\varepsilon^{k+2}(R^{-}_{k+1,k+2}-q^{*})\right)}_{\textnormal{(SK overlap)}}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\beta^{2}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\nu_{s}\left(F\,\varepsilon^{\ell}(M_{\ell}^{-}-m^{*})\right)-k\,\nu_{s}\left(F\,\varepsilon^{k+1}(M_{k+1}^{-}-m^{*})\right)\right]}_{\textnormal{(CW drift)}}.

Proof.

Differentiate
\nu_{s}(F)=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\langle F\rangle_{s}
with respect to
s
.
Using
\mathcal{G}_{s}
for the
s
-dependent part of the Hamiltonian in the cavity interpolation gives

\mathcal{G}_{s}(\rho,\varepsilon):=\varepsilon\Big[\sqrt{s}\,\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leqslant n-1}g_{i}\rho_{i}+\sqrt{1-s}\,\beta\sqrt{q^{*}}\,Z+\beta^{2}\big((1-s)m^{*}+sM^{-}(\rho)\big)\Big],

so that
\beta H_{n,s}=\beta H_{n-1}+\varepsilon y_{n}+\mathcal{G}_{s}
.
The standard Gibbs differentiation identity (using the division rule) gives

\frac{d}{ds}\langle F\rangle_{s}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\Big\langle F\,\partial_{s}\mathcal{G}_{s}(\rho^{\ell},\varepsilon^{\ell})\Big\rangle_{s}-\Big\langle F\Big\rangle_{s}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\Big\langle\partial_{s}\mathcal{G}_{s}(\rho^{\ell},\varepsilon^{\ell})\Big\rangle_{s}.

Now split
\partial_{s}\mathcal{G}_{s}
into the Gaussian SK part and the CW drift:

\partial_{s}\mathcal{G}_{s}(\rho,\varepsilon)=\varepsilon\left[\frac{\beta}{2\sqrt{s\,n}}\sum_{i\leqslant n-1}g_{i}\rho_{i}-\frac{\beta\sqrt{q^{*}}}{2\sqrt{1-s}}\,Z+\beta^{2}(M^{-}(\rho)-m^{*})\right].

The SK contribution.
For the Gaussian part, apply
[
97
, Lemma 1.1]
exactly as in
[
109
, Proof of Lemma 1.6.3]
:
this produces the “SK overlap part” contribution in (
5.17
), written
with replicas
k+1,k+2
using the identity

\langle F\rangle_{s}\langle G\rangle_{s}=\langle F\,G(\sigma^{k+1})\rangle_{s}
.

The CW drift contribution.
For the CW drift term
\varepsilon\beta^{2}(M^{-}(\rho)-m^{*})
, no Gaussian analysis techniques are utilized:
the magnetization terms contribute the covariance of
F
with
\sum_{\ell\leqslant k}\varepsilon^{\ell}(M^{-}_{\ell}-m^{*})
.
Using the same replica-augmentation trick to express
\langle F\rangle_{s}\langle\varepsilon(M-m^{*})\rangle_{s}=\left\langle FG(\sigma^{k+1})\right\rangle_{s}

yields the “CW drift part” in (
5.17
).
∎

Stability of bounded functions along the cavity interpolation

Pinning down the precise limiting estimates for various tracial statistics reduced to replica form will require invoking magnetization and overlap concentration along the
entire
interpolation
s\in[0,1]
. Furthermore, various other “polynomial-in-finite-number-of-replicas” type statistics will be “stable” along the cavity interpolation. Showing this will follow a standard template:

(1)

Prove the required estimate at
s=1
via an argument that uses site-exchangeability and explicit decoupling arguments, and

(2)

Invoke “stability” of
\nu_{1}(F)
by using the fact
|\nu^{\prime}_{s}(F)|\leqslant C(\beta,s)\nu_{1}(F)
.

We begin by showing that overlaps and magnetization are concentrated along the entire interpolation.

Lemma 5.7
(Stability of overlap/magnetization concentration along the cavity interpolation)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Fix
t\in[0,T]
and
\beta>0
. Let

\overline{R}^{-}_{ab}:=R^{-}_{ab}-q^{*},\qquad\overline{M}^{-}_{a}:=M^{-}_{a}-m^{*},

for any replica indices
a,b\in\{1,\dots,K\}
.
Assume, for every
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
there exist constants
C^{(R)}_{p},C^{(M)}_{p}<\infty
such that

(5.18)

\sup_{a<b\leqslant K}\ \nu_{1}\big(|\overline{R}^{-}_{ab}|^{p}\big)\ \leqslant\ C^{(R)}_{p}\,n^{-p/2},\qquad\sup_{a\leqslant K}\ \nu_{1}\big(|\overline{M}^{-}_{a}|^{p}\big)\ \leqslant\ C^{(M)}_{p}\,n^{-p/2}.

Then, for every
s\in[0,1]
,

(5.19)

\sup_{a<b\leqslant K}\ \nu_{s}\big(|\overline{R}^{-}_{ab}|^{p}\big)\ \leqslant\ e^{24\beta^{2}}\,C^{(R)}_{p}\,n^{-p/2},\qquad\sup_{a\leqslant K}\ \nu_{s}\big(|\overline{M}^{-}_{a}|^{p}\big)\ \leqslant\ e^{8\beta^{2}}\,C^{(M)}_{p}\,n^{-p/2}.

Proof.

We use
Lemma
5.6
and the elementary bounds

(5.20)

|\overline{R}^{-}_{ab}|\leqslant 2,\qquad|\overline{M}^{-}_{a}|\leqslant 2,\qquad|\varepsilon^{\ell}|\leqslant 1\,.

We treat overlaps and magnetizations separately.

Stability of the overlaps along the interpolation.
Fix
a<b
