.

A.    Replica identities and higher-order remainder terms

The goal of this section is three-fold:

(1)

To collect a lemma which basically shows that the diagonals of the
\mathsf{GOE}(n)
matrix plays no role in Gibbs averages by using the fact that
\sigma^{2}_{i}=1
, and

(2)

Collect various rewrites of tracial terms that show up in the covariance estimates so that the cavity estimates can be applied in a straight-forward manner.

(3)

Give
O(1)
bounds for various higher-order moment terms that arise after applying Gaussian integration–by-parts to remainder terms in
\mathcal{E}_{A}
, as well as a stand-alone lemma that controls the rank-1 term in
\mathcal{E}_{A}
using similar estimates.

A.1.
Independence of the Gibbs covariance from diagonal entries

While
A\sim\mathsf{GOE}(n)
has diagonal entries with variance
2/n
, it is easy to show that any Gibbs statistics for the planted SK model with SL tilt is “independent” of the Gibbs because of the fact that
\sigma^{2}_{i}=1
.

Lemma A.1
(Diagonal GOE entries do not affect Gibbs averages)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
A
be symmetric and consider the planted SK Hamiltonian

\beta H_{n}(\sigma)=\frac{\beta}{2}\langle\sigma,A\sigma\rangle+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2n}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sigma_{k}\Big)^{2}+\langle y_{t},\sigma\rangle,\qquad\sigma\in\{-1,+1\}^{n}.

Let
A^{\circ}:=A-\diag(A)
be the matrix obtained by zeroing the diagonal of
A
.
Then, for every bounded observable
F:\{-1,+1\}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
,

(A.1)

\langle F\rangle_{A,y_{t},\beta}=\langle F\rangle_{A^{\circ},y_{t},\beta}.

In particular, the magnetization vector
m=\langle\sigma\rangle
and covariance matrix

P=\mathop{{}\bm{\mathrm{Cov}}}(G_{A,y_{t},\beta})
depend on
A
only through its off-diagonal entries:

m=m(A^{\circ},y_{t}),\qquad P=P(A^{\circ},y_{t}).

Consequently, for every
i\in[n]
and every component
m_{k}
and
P_{k\ell}
,

\partial_{A_{ii}}m_{k}=0,\qquad\partial_{A_{ii}}P_{k\ell}=0,

and the same holds for any matrix/function built from
m
and
P
.

Proof.

Since
A
is symmetric,

\langle\sigma,A\sigma\rangle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}A_{ij}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}=2\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}A_{ij}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{ii}\sigma_{i}^{2}.

But
\sigma_{i}^{2}\equiv 1
, so

\frac{\beta}{2}\langle\sigma,A\sigma\rangle=\beta\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}A_{ij}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}+\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{ii}.

The last term
\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i}A_{ii}
is independent of
\sigma
. Therefore, writing

\Phi(\sigma;A^{\circ},y_{t}):=\beta\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2n}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sigma_{k}\Big)^{2}+\langle y_{t},\sigma\rangle,

we have

e^{\beta H_{n}(\sigma)}=\exp\Big(\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{ii}\Big)\cdot e^{\Phi(\sigma;A^{\circ},y_{t})}.

Therefore, the partition function factorizes as

Z(A,y_{t})=\sum_{\sigma}e^{\beta H_{n}(\sigma)}=\exp\Big(\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{ii}\Big)\sum_{\sigma}e^{\Phi(\sigma;A^{\circ},y_{t})}=\exp\Big(\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{ii}\Big)\,Z(A^{\circ},y_{t}).

This implies that for any bounded
F
,

\langle F\rangle_{A,y_{t},\beta}=\frac{1}{Z(A,y_{t})}\sum_{\sigma}F(\sigma)e^{\beta H_{n}(\sigma)}=\frac{1}{Z(A^{\circ},y_{t})}\sum_{\sigma}F(\sigma)e^{\Phi(\sigma;A^{\circ},y_{t})}=\langle F\rangle_{A^{\circ},y_{t},\beta},

which proves (
A.1
). Since the RHS does not depend on
(A_{ii})
, all derivatives

\partial_{A_{ii}}\langle F\rangle
vanish identically. Applying this with
F(\sigma)=\sigma_{k}
and
F(\sigma)=\sigma_{k}\sigma_{\ell}

gives
\partial_{A_{ii}}m_{k}=0
and
\partial_{A_{ii}}P_{k\ell}=0
. The same holds for any composition

D(m),c(m),V(m),S(m)
.
∎

A.2.
Replica simplifications for cross terms via tracial identities and index algebra

Below is a helper lemma that accounts for cancellations in the multi-overlaps that come from an expansion using replica-based identities for the
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[PA^{2}P]\right]
term. This is a (minor) generalization of the computations in
[
6
, Pg. 6 to 9]
for the
\mathrm{III}
term
[
6
, Pg. 6]
to account for the case where the covariance is (potentially) uncentered.

Lemma A.2
(Exact
P
-pairing decomposition for
\partial_{ij}P_{ab}
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Fix
i<j
. For all
a,b\in\{1,\dots,n\}
,

(A.2)

\partial_{ij}P_{ab}=\beta\Big(P_{ai}P_{bj}+P_{aj}P_{bi}\Big)+\beta\Big(m_{i}\,T_{abj}+m_{j}\,T_{abi}+\Gamma_{abij}\Big).

Proof.

Start from the exact Gibbs derivative identity that follows by the division rule

\partial_{ij}\langle F\rangle=\beta\Big(\langle F\,\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-\langle F\rangle\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle\Big)=\beta\,\langle F\,(\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}-\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle)\rangle.

Applying this to
F=\widetilde{\sigma}_{a}\widetilde{\sigma}_{b}
and recalling
P_{ab}=\langle\widetilde{\sigma}_{a}\widetilde{\sigma}_{b}\rangle
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}P_{ab}

\displaystyle=\partial_{ij}\langle\sigma_{a}\sigma_{b}\rangle-(\partial_{ij}m_{a})m_{b}-m_{a}(\partial_{ij}m_{b})

(A.3)

\displaystyle=\beta\Big(\langle\sigma_{a}\sigma_{b}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-\langle\sigma_{a}\sigma_{b}\rangle\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle\Big)-\beta m_{b}\Big(\langle\sigma_{a}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-m_{a}\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle\Big)-\beta m_{a}\Big(\langle\sigma_{b}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-m_{b}\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle\Big).

Note that, expanding
\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}=(m_{i}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})(m_{j}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})=m_{i}m_{j}+m_{i}\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}+m_{j}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}
.
Also
\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle=m_{i}m_{j}+P_{ij}
.
Inserting these into (
A.2
) cancels the
m_{i}m_{j}
term to finally give

\partial_{ij}P_{ab}=\beta\Big(m_{i}\langle\widetilde{\sigma}_{a}\widetilde{\sigma}_{b}\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\rangle+m_{j}\langle\widetilde{\sigma}_{a}\widetilde{\sigma}_{b}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\rangle+\langle\widetilde{\sigma}_{a}\widetilde{\sigma}_{b}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\rangle-P_{ab}P_{ij}\Big).

Finally, note that the definition of
\Gamma_{abij}
gives

\langle\widetilde{\sigma}_{a}\widetilde{\sigma}_{b}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\rangle-P_{ab}P_{ij}=P_{ai}P_{bj}+P_{aj}P_{bi}+\Gamma_{abij}\,,

and substituting this into the expression for
\partial_{ij}P_{ab}
yields (
A.2
).
∎

Lemma A.3
(Exact replica representations for tracial functions)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
G
be the Gibbs measure,
P=\mathrm{Cov}(G)
and
T=\diag(P)
.
For replicas
\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\sigma^{3},\sigma^{4}\sim G^{\otimes 4}
, define

a_{i}:=(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})(\sigma_{i}^{3}-\sigma_{i}^{4}),\qquad f:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i},\qquad\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{\ell}:=\frac{\sigma_{i}^{\ell}-m_{i}}{\sqrt{1-m_{i}^{2}}},\qquad\widehat{f}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{1}-\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{2})(\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{3}-\widehat{\sigma}_{i}^{4}).

Also, define the “diagonally weighted” rectangular sum

f^{\mathrm{diag}}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{ii}\,a_{i}.

Then, the following tracial equalities hold conditioned on any choice of
\{A_{ij}\}_{i,j}
,

(A.4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\,\langle f^{2}\rangle,

(A.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\,\langle f\widehat{f}\rangle,

(A.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle a_{i}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{ii}^{2}.

(A.7)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[TP^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\,\langle f\,f^{\mathrm{diag}}\rangle,

(A.8)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[TPDP]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\,\langle f^{\mathrm{diag}}\,\widehat{f}\rangle.

Proof.

For (
A.4
), note that

P_{ij}=\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-m_{i}m_{j}=\frac{1}{2}\langle(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})(\sigma_{j}^{1}-\sigma_{j}^{2})\rangle.

This immediately gives

P_{ij}^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\langle(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})(\sigma_{j}^{1}-\sigma_{j}^{2})\rangle\langle(\sigma_{i}^{3}-\sigma_{i}^{4})(\sigma_{j}^{3}-\sigma_{j}^{4})\rangle,

and by the replica product identity,

P_{ij}^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\langle(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})(\sigma_{i}^{3}-\sigma_{i}^{4})(\sigma_{j}^{1}-\sigma_{j}^{2})(\sigma_{j}^{3}-\sigma_{j}^{4})\rangle.

Summing over
i,j
gives

\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]=\sum_{i,j}P_{ij}^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\Big\langle\Big(\sum_{i}a_{i}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j}a_{j}\Big)\Big\rangle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\langle f^{2}\rangle.

Using
D_{jj}=\frac{1}{1-m_{j}^{2}}
, one obtains

\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]=\sum_{i,j}P_{ij}^{2}D_{jj}.

Insert the same replica representation for
P_{ij}^{2}
as above and pull
D_{jj}
inside by rewriting

D_{jj}(\sigma_{j}^{1}-\sigma_{j}^{2})(\sigma_{j}^{3}-\sigma_{j}^{4})=\frac{(\sigma_{j}^{1}-m_{j})-(\sigma_{j}^{2}-m_{j})}{\sqrt{1-m_{j}^{2}}}\cdot\frac{(\sigma_{j}^{3}-m_{j})-(\sigma_{j}^{4}-m_{j})}{\sqrt{1-m_{j}^{2}}}.

This is exactly
(\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{1}-\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2})(\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{3}-\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{4})
.
Therefore

\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]=\frac{1}{4}\Big\langle\Big(\sum_{i}a_{i}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j}\widehat{a}_{j}\Big)\Big\rangle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\langle f\widehat{f}\rangle,

which proves (
A.5
).

For (
A.6
), condition on disorder and use the independence of the replicas under the Gibbs to obtain

\langle a_{i}^{2}\rangle=\langle(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})^{2}\rangle\;\langle(\sigma_{i}^{3}-\sigma_{i}^{4})^{2}\rangle.

However, using the fact that
(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})^{2}=2(1-\sigma_{i}^{1}\sigma_{i}^{2})
we simplify further to

\langle(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})^{2}\rangle=2\big(1-\langle\sigma_{i}^{1}\sigma_{i}^{2}\rangle\big)=2(1-m_{i}^{2})=2P_{ii}.

Since
\langle a_{i}^{2}\rangle=4P_{ii}^{2}
, summing and dividing by
n
gives (
A.6
).

The expressions for (
A.7
) and (
A.8
) follow via similar algebraic simplifications.

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}[TP^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}=\sum_{i,j}P_{ii}P_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i,j}P_{ii}\left\langle(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})(\sigma_{j}^{1}-\sigma_{j}^{2})\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i,j}P_{ii}\left\langle(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{2})(\sigma_{i}^{3}-\sigma_{i}^{4})(\sigma_{j}^{1}-\sigma_{j}^{2})(\sigma_{j}^{3}-\sigma_{j}^{4})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left\langle\left(\sum_{i}P_{ii}a_{i}\right)\left(\sum_{j}a_{j}\right)\right\rangle=\frac{1}{4}\,\langle(nf^{\mathrm{diag}})\,(nf)\rangle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\,\langle f\,f^{\mathrm{diag}}\rangle\,,

and

\mathsf{Tr}[TPDP]=\sum_{i,j}P_{ii}P_{ij}^{2}\frac{1}{1-m_{j}^{2}}=\frac{1}{4}\Big\langle\Big(\sum_{i}P_{ii}a_{i}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j}\widehat{a}_{j}\Big)\Big\rangle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}\,\langle f^{\mathrm{diag}}\,\widehat{f}\rangle\,,

where the proof is identical to the previous step, with one
a_{j}
replaced by
\widehat{a}_{j}
. ∎

Lemma A.4
(Index trace identities for
P
and
D
)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
m\in(-1,1)^{n}
and let
P\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be symmetric with

P_{ij}=\langle\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\rangle-\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\langle\sigma_{j}\rangle,\qquad P_{ii}=1-m_{i}^{2},\qquad D:=\mathrm{diag}\Big(\frac{1}{1-m_{1}^{2}},\dots,\frac{1}{1-m_{n}^{2}}\Big)=\mathrm{diag}\Big(\frac{1}{P_{11}},\dots,\frac{1}{P_{nn}}\Big).

Then the following identities hold:

(i)

\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}P_{ij}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{ii}^{2}+2\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}^{2}.

(ii)

\mathsf{Tr}[P]^{2}=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{ii}\Big)^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{ii}^{2}+2\sum_{i<j}P_{ii}P_{jj}.

Equivalently,

\sum_{i<j}P_{ii}P_{jj}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\mathsf{Tr}(P)^{2}-\sum_{i}P_{ii}^{2}\Big).

(iii)

\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}P_{ij}D_{jj}P_{ji}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{jj}\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{ij}^{2}=\mathsf{Tr}(P)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{jj}\sum_{i\neq j}P_{ij}^{2}.

Therefore,

(A.9)

\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]-\mathsf{Tr}[P]=\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{jj}\sum_{i\neq j}P_{ij}^{2}=\sum_{i<j}(D_{ii}+D_{jj})P_{ij}^{2}.

Proof.

The proof for (i) and (ii) are immediate expansions. For (iii), since
D
is diagonal,

\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]=\sum_{i,j}P_{ij}D_{jj}P_{ji}=\sum_{j}D_{jj}\sum_{i}P_{ij}^{2}.

Splitting the inner sum into
i=j
and
i\neq j
components gives that

\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]=\sum_{j}D_{jj}P_{jj}^{2}+\sum_{j}D_{jj}\sum_{i\neq j}P_{ij}^{2}.

However, since
D_{jj}P_{jj}^{2}=P_{jj}
, this simplifies to give

\mathsf{Tr}[PDP]=\sum_{j}P_{jj}+\sum_{j}D_{jj}\sum_{i\neq j}P_{ij}^{2}=\mathsf{Tr}(P)+\sum_{j}D_{jj}\sum_{i\neq j}P_{ij}^{2}.

Finally, rewrite the last double sum symmetrically yields

\sum_{j}D_{jj}\sum_{i\neq j}P_{ij}^{2}=\sum_{i<j}\big(D_{ii}+D_{jj}\big)P_{ij}^{2}\,,

which is the desired identity.
∎

A.3.
Bounds for the
\mathrm{HO}
terms

In this subsection we will state a lemma that will tersely illustrate the computations showing that each
\mathrm{HO}_{i}
term is
O(1)
. We do this using (rather repetitively) the same replica rewrites and summing over rectangular sums to turn every
\mathrm{HO}_{i}
term to be equivalent to
\mathsf{span}\left\{\nu(fG),\nu(f_{\alpha}f_{\beta}f_{\gamma}f_{\delta})\right\}
or a term that decays even faster, and then applying some combination of
Proposition
5.21
or Hölder’s inequalities in conjunction with
Theorem
5.1
and
Lemma
5.16
.

Lemma A.5
(
O(1)
bounds for the
\mathrm{HO}
terms)
.
p_{p_{p_{p}}}

Let
\mathrm{HO}_{i}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,16\}
be as defined in
Lemma
4.15
. Then,

\mathrm{HO}_{i}=O(1)\,,

for every
i
.

Proof 45
.

We prove the bounds for every term in chronological order. Each proof (roughly) follows the same pattern – use replica rewrites for the centered third-moment or fourth cumulant terms, perform the inner summation, symmetrize over the outer sums, and then use some combination of
Theorem
5.1
,
Lemma
5.16
or
Proposition
5.21
in conjunction with Hölder’s inequality to deal with “grouped sum” terms, and deal with the diagonal terms using established bounds on trace powers of
P
.

\mathrm{HO}_{1}
term.
Using replica rewrites for the third-centered moment and symmetrization of sums

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}T_{ikj}\right]=\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{i}\sum_{k}\left\langle\Delta^{12}_{k}\Delta^{34}_{j}f_{1234}\Delta^{56}_{i}\Delta^{57}_{k}\Delta^{56}_{j}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=C(\beta)n^{2}\nu((R_{57}-R_{67})f_{3456}f_{1234}f_{1257})-C^{\prime}(\beta)\nu\left(f_{1234}f_{1257}\left(\sum_{i}\sigma^{7}_{i}(\Delta^{56}_{i})^{2}\Delta^{34}_{i}\right)\right)\,.

The first term is shown to be
O(1)
by a
(4,4,4,4)
-Hölder’s in conjunction with
Theorem
5.1
and
Lemma
5.16
, while the second term is
O(1)
by observing that
|\sum_{i}\sigma^{7}_{i}(\Delta^{56}_{i})^{2}\Delta^{34}_{i}|\leqslant 80n
and using Cauchy–Schwarz in conjunction with
Lemma
5.16
.

\mathrm{HO}_{2}
term.
Using replica rewrites with
T_{iki}=-2m_{i}P_{ik}
and symmetrization of sums again gives

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}m_{j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}T_{iki}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[-m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{3}m+\mathsf{Tr}[\diag(m^{2})P^{3}]\right]\,.

Now
|m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{3}m|\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}\leqslant n\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]
which immediately implies that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{3}m=O(1)
via an application of (
4.2
). For the second term, note that the proof of
Lemma
4.15
already demonstrates it is
O(1)
.

\mathrm{HO}_{3}
term.
Using the fact that
\Gamma_{ikij}=-2P_{ki}P_{ij}-2m_{i}T_{ikj}
the term simplifies down to

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}\Gamma_{ikij}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}P_{ki}P_{ij}\right]+\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}T_{ikj}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]\right]+\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\right]+\mathrm{HO}_{1}\,.

The first term is
O(1)
immediately by using (
4.2
), while the third term is
O(1)
by our previous bound. For the second term, observe that
0\leqslant P_{ii}\leqslant 1
and so
\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]
, at which point
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\right]=O(1)
by (
4.2
).

\mathrm{HO}_{4}
term.
Using the identities that
T_{k\ell j}=\left\langle\Delta^{12}_{k}\Delta^{13}_{\ell}\Delta^{12}_{j}\right\rangle
,
P_{\ell j}=\left\langle\Delta^{45}_{\ell}\Delta^{45}_{j}\right\rangle
and
P_{ik}=\left\langle\Delta^{67}_{i}\Delta^{67}_{k}\right\rangle
we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k,\ell}P_{ik}P_{\ell j}T_{k\ell j}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}\left\langle\sigma^{8}_{i}\Delta^{67}_{i}\Delta^{67}_{k}\Delta^{12}_{k}\Delta^{45}_{j}\Delta^{12}_{j}f_{1345}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=C(\beta)n^{2}\nu\left((R_{68}-R_{78})f_{1245}f_{1267}f_{1345}\right)-C(\beta)\nu(B^{n}f_{1345}f_{1267})\,,

where
B^{n}
is some function of the spins that is absolutely bounded by
80n
. Both these terms can be shown to be
O(1)
as they are of exactly the same form as the terms that
\mathrm{HO}_{1}
evaluates to.

\mathrm{HO}_{5}
term.
For the fifth term, we use the fact that
\Gamma_{k\ell ij}=\left\langle\Delta^{12}_{k}\Delta^{13}_{\ell}\Delta^{14}_{i}\Delta^{15}_{j}\right\rangle-P_{k\ell}P_{ij}-P_{ki}P_{\ell j}-P_{kj}P_{\ell i}
and symmetrize sums to get an expression involving bulk deviations products, and three auxiliary trace power terms. More specifically,

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k,\ell}P_{ik}P_{\ell j}\Gamma_{k\ell ij}\right]=C(\beta)n^{2}\nu\left(f_{1267}f_{1389}f_{1589}f_{1467}\right)-C(\beta)\nu\left(B^{n}f_{1267}f_{1389}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}(P^{3})_{ij}-\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}-\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})^{2}_{ij}\right]\,,

where
B^{n}
is again some function of the spins that is absolutely bounded by
100n
. The first two terms are bounded by
O(1)
using the same strategy used on the prior terms, while the remaining terms can be symmetrized and written as

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}(P^{3})_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})_{ii}(P^{2})_{jj}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}(P^{2}_{ii})^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}(P^{2})^{2}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}^{2}\,,

which can be bounded by observing that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]]=O(1)
by (
4.2
), that
|\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}|\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]
and so
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]]=O(1)
by (
4.2
), that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]^{2}\right]=\frac{n^{2}}{16}\nu(f_{1234}^{2}f_{5678}^{2})=O(1)
, and that
\sum_{i}(P^{2})^{2}_{ii}\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]
which immediately yields
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i}(P^{2})^{2}_{ii}\leqslant O(1)
by (
4.2
). Putting all the bounds together immediately gives
\mathrm{HO}_{5}=O(1)
.

\mathrm{HO}_{6}
,
\mathrm{HO}_{7}
and
\mathrm{HO}_{8}
terms.
The proof for this is (essentially) an easier variant of the proof used to bound
R_{2}
in
Lemma
4.15
. Observe that
0\leqslant P_{ii}+P_{jj}\leqslant 2
, and then note that

\displaystyle\mathrm{HO}_{6}+\mathrm{HO}_{7}+\mathrm{HO}_{8}=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})\left(m_{i}\sum_{k}P_{kj}T_{ikj}+m_{j}\sum_{k}P_{kj}T_{iki}+\sum_{k}P_{kj}\Gamma_{ikij}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}P_{kj}(m_{i}T_{ikj}+m_{j}T_{iki}+P_{kj}\Gamma_{ikij})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})\left(\sum_{k}P_{kj}(-m_{i}T_{ikj}-2m_{i}m_{j}P_{ik}-2P_{ik}P_{ij})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})m_{i}m_{j}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]+\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}\right]+\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{i}(P_{ii}+P_{jj})\sum_{k}P_{jk}T_{ikj}\right]\,.

We control each of these terms individually. For the first one, after symmetrization of sums, we obtain the following

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})m_{i}m_{j}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m^{\mathsf{T}}E_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}[P]P^{2}m\right]-\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}m^{2}_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]\,.

Since
\sum_{i}m_{i}(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii})\leqslant_{|m_{i}|\leqslant 1}\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii}\leqslant_{|P_{ii}|\leqslant 1}\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}=\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]
, we have
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}m_{i}(P_{ii}(P^{2})_{ii})\right]=O(1)
by invoking (
4.2
). Similarly,
m^{\mathsf{T}}E_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}[P]^{1/2}P^{2}E_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}[P]^{1/2}m\leqslant\left\lVert{P^{2}}\right\rVert_{F}\left\lVert{E_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}[P]^{1/2}m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}
. A
(1,\infty)
-Hölder’s gives that
\left\lVert{E_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}[P]^{1/2}m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}\leqslant_{m^{2}_{i}\leqslant 1}\mathsf{Tr}[P]\leqslant n
and
\left\lVert{P^{2}}\right\rVert_{F}=\sqrt{\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]}
. This immediately implies that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[m^{\mathsf{T}}E_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}[P]P^{2}m]\leqslant\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sqrt{\mathsf{Tr}[P^{4}]}\right]
which is
O(1)
by Jensen’s in conjunction with (
4.2
).

For the second term, observe that

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ii}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]-\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii})^{2}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\right]-\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii})^{2}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]\,.

Using the fact that
0\leqslant P_{ii}\leqslant 1
and
P\succeq 0
(again) gives that
\left|\sum_{i}P_{ii}(P^{3})_{ii}\right|\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]
which immediately yields
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ii}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ij}\right]=O(1)
by using (
4.2
). For the second term, we use a similar strategy with the invocation that
(P_{ii})^{2}\leqslant 1
in conjunction with (
4.2
). This gives
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P_{ii}+P_{jj})P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ji}\right]=O(1)
.

For the last term, the following rewrite is immediately obvious using the replica identities and some algebra once we isolate the sum over the
k
index. Specifically,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k}P_{jk}T_{ikj}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\left\langle(\sigma_{i}-m_{i})(\sigma_{j}-m_{j})\sum_{k}P_{jk}(\sigma_{k}-m_{k})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{CS}}\sqrt{\left\langle(\sigma_{i}-m_{i})^{2}(\sigma_{j}-m_{j})^{2}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left\langle\left(\sum_{k}P_{kj}(\sigma_{k}-m_{k})\right)\left(\sum_{\ell}P_{j\ell}(\sigma_{\ell}-m_{\ell})\right)\right\rangle^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant_{|P_{ij}|\leqslant 4,\,P_{k\ell}=(\sigma_{k}-m_{k})(\sigma_{\ell}-m_{\ell})}4\sqrt{(P^{3})_{jj}}\,.

Using the fact that
|m_{i}(P_{ii}+P_{jj})|\leqslant 4
and Jensen’s inequality, we immediately obtain that

\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{i}(P_{ii}+P_{jj})\sum_{k}P_{jk}T_{ikj}\right]\leqslant\frac{C(\beta)}{n}\sum_{j}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[(P^{3})_{jj}\right]}\leqslant_{\text{CS}}C(\beta)\sqrt{\frac{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]\right]}{n}}\leqslant_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p3-bound}}}O(1)\,.

\mathrm{HO}_{9}
and
\mathrm{HO}_{10}
terms.
The proofs for both these terms are very similar to the bound for
\mathrm{HO}_{8}
, so we keep it concise. Since

\displaystyle\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{ij}m_{i}P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ik}T_{ikj}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{CS}}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}m_{i}^{2}(P_{ij})^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}\left(\sum_{k}P_{ik}T_{ijk}\right)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leqslant_{|m_{i}^{2}|\leqslant 1,\,\text{CS}}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}(\sum_{k}(P_{ik})^{2})(\sum_{k}T^{2}_{ijk})\right]}

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p2-bound}},\,|T^{2}_{ijk}|\leqslant 32}C\,\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i,j}(P^{2})_{ii}n\right]}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sqrt{n^{3/2}}\sqrt{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]}\leqslant_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p2-bound}}}O(n^{2})\,,

which immediately implies that
\mathrm{HO}_{9}=O(1)
, since the diagonal term is easily shown to be
O(1/n)
by using the fact that
T_{iki}=-2m_{i}P_{ki}
, and summing over
i=j
gives a bound on the order of
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\right]=O(1/n)
.

For the
\mathrm{HO}_{10}
term, observe that

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{j}P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ki}T_{iki}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m_{m}m_{j}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant_{|m_{i}m_{j}P_{ij}|\leqslant 1}\frac{C^{\prime}(\beta)}{n}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i}(P^{2})_{ii}\right]=_{\text{\eqref{eq:c-p2-bound}}}O(1)\,.

\mathrm{HO}_{11}
term.
The
\mathrm{HO}_{11}
term involves a fourth cumulant, and requires bounding sub-terms that follow from replica rewrites. Fortunately, a bound on the absolute value of the fourth cumulant (akin to the one used to bound
|T_{ikj}|\leqslant C
) combines with a
\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}
inequality to yield the desired bound.

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ik}\Gamma_{ikij}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant_{|\Gamma_{ikij}|\leqslant C}C\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}|P_{ij}||P_{ik}|\leqslant C\sum_{i}\left(\sum_{j}|P_{ij}|\sum_{k}|P_{ik}|\right)

\displaystyle=C\sum_{i}\left(\sum_{j}|P_{ij}|\right)^{2}\leqslant_{\text{CS}}Cn\sum_{i}\sum_{j}(P_{ij})^{2}=Cn\mathsf{Tr}[P^{2}]\,,

at which point (
4.2
) immediately implies that
\frac{4\beta^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[P_{ij}\sum_{k}P_{ik}\Gamma_{ikij}\right]
.

\mathrm{HO}_{12}
term.
Using the fact that
T_{jii}=-2m_{i}P_{ij}
and some trace algebra, the bound follows rather straightforwardly as

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}T_{jii}\sum_{k}m_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}

\displaystyle=-2\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}m_{i}m_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}P_{ij}\,.

Since the joint law of
(A,y_{t})
(with fixed plant
x_{0}=\mathbf{1}_{n}
) is invariant under the permutation that swaps
i
and
j
, a symmetrization argument immediately implies that

\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}m_{i}m_{k}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{kj}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\neq j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}m_{i}m_{k}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{kj}\right]\,.

At this point, simple linear algebra yields that

\displaystyle\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k}m_{i}m_{k}P_{ij}(P^{2})_{kj}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{3}m-m^{\mathsf{T}}\diag(P)P^{2}m\right]\,.

Using the fact that
\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}\leqslant n
and
\left\lVert P^{3}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{op}}\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]
, we immediately obtain that the first term is
O(1)
by (
4.2
), and the second term is small because

\displaystyle|m^{\mathsf{T}}P^{2}\diag(P)m|

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\text{CS}}\left\lVert{\diag(P)m}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert{P^{2}m}\right\rVert_{2}\leqslant_{\diag(P)\preceq I}\sqrt{n}\left\lVert{P^{2}m}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\left\lVert P\right\rVert_{\mathsf{op}}^{4}\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}}\leqslant_{\left\lVert P^{4}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{op}}\leqslant(\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}])^{4/3}}n\mathsf{Tr}[P^{3}]^{2/3}\,.

At this point, invoking Jensen’s (with the concavity of
x^{2/3}
for
x\geqslant 0
) along with (
4.2
) immediately yields that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[m^{\mathsf{T}}\diag(P)P^{2}m]=O(n^{-1/3})
.

\mathrm{HO}_{13}
term.
The bound for this term follows by straightforward replica algebra on using the identities that
(P^{2})_{jk}=\frac{n}{4}\left\langle f_{1234}\Delta^{12}\Delta^{34}_{k}\right\rangle
and
T_{jik}=\left\langle\Delta^{56}_{j}\Delta^{57}_{i}\Delta^{58}_{k}\right\rangle
along with symmetrization over the
i<j
sum to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}m_{i}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{jk}T_{jik}=C(\beta)n^{4}\left\langle(R_{59}-R_{79})f_{1256}f_{3458}f_{1234}\right\rangle-C^{\prime}(\beta)n^{2}\left\langle B^{n}_{i}f_{1234}f_{3458}\right\rangle\,,

where
B^{n}_{i}
is a function absolutely bounded by
Cn
after summing over the
i
indices. At this point, taking
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\cdot\right]
and using
(4,4,4,4)
-Hölder’s for the first term (after separating it into its 2 constituent bulk centered terms) with
Theorem
5.1
and
Lemma
5.16
shows the first term is
O(1)
, and using Cauchy–Schwarz along with
|B^{n}_{i}|\leqslant Cn
and the same concentration shows the second term is also
O(1)
.

\mathrm{HO}_{14}
term.
Note that
\Gamma_{jiki}=-2m_{i}T_{ijk}-2P_{ji}P_{ik}
. This immediately implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{jk}\Gamma_{jiki}

\displaystyle=-2\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}m_{i}T_{ijk}-2\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}P_{ji}P_{ik}\,.

Note that
\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2}}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k}(P^{2})_{kj}m_{i}T_{ijk}\right]=O(1)
since the term reduces (up to an index re-shuffle) the
\mathrm{HO}_{13}
term, and the exact same argument works. For the second term, observe that exchangeability of
i
and
j
under
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}
and
\left\langle\cdot\right\rangle
implies that
